\displaystyle+u^{N}_{k}(t-,x_{1})r\Big(\|u^{N}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{N}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{r-1}\left|\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}-\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq r\Big(\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{r}

\displaystyle\cdot\Big(\left|u^{N}_{k}(t-,x_{1})-u^{N}_{k}(t-,x_{2})\right|+\left|\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}-\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\right|\Big),

where the first inequality follows since for any
a,b\geq 0
and
r\in[1,\infty)
, by the mean value theorem,

|a^{r}-b^{r}|\leq r(a\vee b)^{r-1}|a-b|\leq r(a+b)^{r-1}|a-b|,

(4.63)

and the second inequality follows since
u^{N}_{k}(t-,x_{1})\leq\|u^{N}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}
. For
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
, recall the definition of
U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
4.1
). By (
4.62
), the proof will be complete after establishing that for
T\geq 0
and
r\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{r,T}^{(1)}>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{r}\left|U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{1})-U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{2})\right|\Big]\,dt

(4.64)

\displaystyle\leq C^{(1)}_{r,T}\Big(|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{1/4}+|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{1/2}\Big).

To bound the integrand on the left-hand side of (
4.64
), we observe that by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, for
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{r}\left|U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{1})-U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{2})\right|\Big]

(4.65)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{2r}\left|U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{1})-U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{2})\right|\Big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\left|U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{1})-U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{2})\right|\Big]^{1/2}.

Note that by the definition of
u^{N}_{k}(t,\cdot)
by linear interpolation after (
2.8
), we have that for
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
{\tau}\geq 0
,

\|u^{N}({\tau},x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\leq\|u^{N}({\tau},L_{N}^{-1}\lfloor L_{N}x\rfloor)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{N}({\tau},L_{N}^{-1}(\lfloor L_{N}x\rfloor+1))\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}.

(4.66)

Therefore, there exists
C^{(2)}_{r,T}>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
t\in[0,T]
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{2r}\left|U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{1})-U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{2})\right|\Big]

(4.67)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{2r+1}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2^{2r}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{2r+1}\Big]+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{2r+1}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{4r}\sum_{y\in\{L_{N}^{-1}\lfloor L_{N}x_{1}\rfloor,L_{N}^{-1}(\lfloor L_{N}x_{1}\rfloor+1),L_{N}^{-1}\lfloor L_{N}x_{2}\rfloor,L_{N}^{-1}(\lfloor L_{N}x_{2}\rfloor+1)\}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{2r+1}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C^{(2)}_{r,T},

where the second and third inequalities follow from the elementary inequality

(a+b)^{2r+1}\leq 2^{2r}(a^{2r+1}+b^{2r+1})\quad\forall\,a,b\geq 0,\;\forall\,r\in[1,\infty),

(4.68)

combined with (
4.66
) for the third inequality, and the last inequality follows from Theorem
3.3
. Hence, combining (
4.65
) and (
4.67
), for
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|u^{{{N}}}(t-,x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{r}\left|U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{1})-U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{2})\right|\Big]\,dt

(4.69)

\displaystyle\leq(C^{(2)}_{r,T})^{1/2}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\left|U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{1})-U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{2})\right|\Big]^{1/2}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq(C^{(2)}_{r,T})^{1/2}T^{1/2}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\left|U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{1})-U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x_{2})\right|\Big]\,dt\Bigg)^{1/2},

where the second inequality follows from Jensen’s inequality. Since
(a+b)^{1/2}\leq a^{1/2}+b^{1/2}
for all
a,b\geq 0
, estimate (
4.64
) follows from applying Lemma
4.5
to (
4.69
). Then, by combining (
4.62
) and (
4.64
), the proof is complete.
∎

To finish this section, we will apply Lemma
4.3
to bound the expected density of particles carrying a high number of mutations.

Lemma 4.7
.

Suppose the conditions of Lemma
4.4
hold. For
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, let
\mathcal{I}_{k}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}:\;j\geq k\}
. Then, for any
T\geq 0
,

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\sup_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(t,x)\right]=0.

Proof.

Let
T\geq 0
be fixed. By taking expectations on both sides of (
4.20
) and then applying (
4.21
) from Lemma
4.3
, we get, for all
t\in[0,T]
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,

\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(t,x)\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[P^{N}_{t}U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(0,\cdot)(x)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[A^{N,t,x}_{{\mathcal{I}_{k}}}(t)\right],

(4.70)

where
A^{N,t,x}_{{\mathcal{I}_{k}}}(t)
is given in (
4.22
). Since
f=\left(f_{k}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
satisfies Assumption
4
, we have that
f\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
and there exists a Lebesgue null set
\mathcal{N}\subset\mathbb{R}
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\mathcal{N}}\,\sum_{k\geq n}f_{k}(x)=0.

Therefore, since
\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}
is given by (
2.1
), and since by (
2.8
) and (
4.1
), for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,

U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(0,x)=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j\geq k}{\eta}^{N}_{j}(0,x),

we have

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(0,x)\right]=0.

Hence, by the definition of the action of the semigroup
\{P^{N}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
after (
4.2
), we have for all
T\geq 0
,

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\sup_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[P^{N}_{t}U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(0,\cdot)(x)\right]=0.

Hence, by (
4.70
), it will suffice to verify that for any
T\geq 0
, there exists a sequence of positive real numbers
\left({\epsilon}_{k,T}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
such that
{\epsilon}_{k,T}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
, and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\sup_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[A^{N,t,x}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(t)\right]\leq{\epsilon}_{k,T}.

(4.71)

Applying the expression for
A^{N,t,x}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(t)
given in (
4.22
) in the statement of Lemma
4.3
and the definition of
F_{k}(u)
in (
2.12
), noting that
q_{-}
is non-negative by Assumption
3
, and recalling that by Assumption
2
(iii), the sequence
(s_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is monotonically non-increasing, we conclude that for
t\in[0,T]
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[A^{N,t,x}_{\mathcal{I}_{k}}(t)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{t}p^{N}(t-{\tau},y-x)\Big(s_{k}+\mathds{1}_{\{k\geq 1\}}s_{k-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{j\geq k}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[q_{+}\left(\|u^{N}({\tau}-,y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\right)\Big(u^{N}_{j}({\tau}-,y)+\mathds{1}_{\{j\geq 1\}}u^{N}_{j-1}({\tau}-,y)\Big)\Big]\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{q_{+},T}\Big(s_{k}+\mathds{1}_{\{k\geq 1\}}s_{k-1}\Big)\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{t}p^{N}(t-{\tau},y-x)\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle=t\Big(s_{k}+\mathds{1}_{\{k\geq 1\}}s_{k-1}\Big),

where for the second inequality we applied Theorem
3.3
, and for the last identity we used (
4.42
). Therefore, since by Assumption
2
(iv) we have
s_{k}\rightarrow 0
as
k\rightarrow\infty
, (
4.71
) follows, which completes the proof.
∎

5
Tightness

Under Assumptions
2
,
3
and
4
, for
N\in\mathbb{N}
, defining
\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}
as in (
2.1
), let
({\eta}^{N}(t))_{t\geq 0}
with
{\eta}^{N}(0)=\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}
denote the
\mathcal{S}^{N}
-valued càdlàg Markov process formally defined in Theorem
3.2
, and recall the stochastic process
(u^{N}(t))_{t\geq 0}
defined in (
2.8
) and (
2.9
). Moreover, recall that as stated after (
2.10
),
(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)
is a complete and separable metric space. Therefore, the space
\mathscr{D}\left([0,\infty);(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)
is also a complete and separable metric space when equipped with the Skorokhod metric, which generates the
{J}_{1}
-topology (see e.g.
[
29
, Theorem 3.5.6]
). The goal of this section is to verify the following property of
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, the sequence of processes
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight in
\mathscr{D}\left([0,\infty);(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)
.

To prove Proposition
5.1
, our strategy will be to apply Jakubowski’s criterion
[
44
, Theorem 3.1]
. According to this criterion, tightness of
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
will follow from Propositions
5.2
and
5.3
below.

Proposition 5.2

(Compact containment condition)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for any
T\geq 0
and
{\varepsilon}>0
, there exists a compact set
\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}\subset\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
such that

\inf_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left(u^{N}(t)\in\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}\;\forall t\in[0,T]\right)\geq 1-{\varepsilon}.

Proposition 5.3

(Tightness of evaluations)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, there exists
\mathbb{F}\subset\mathscr{C}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}};\mathbb{R})
, i.e. a family of real-valued continuous functions on
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

\mathbb{F}
separates points in
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
, i.e. for any
u,v\in\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
with
u\neq v
, there exists
{\varphi}\in\mathbb{F}
such that
{\varphi}(u)\neq{\varphi}(v)
;

(ii)

\mathbb{F}
is closed under addition, i.e. if
{\varphi},\tilde{{\varphi}}\in\mathbb{F}
, then
{\varphi}+\tilde{{\varphi}}\in\mathbb{F}
;

and such that the sequence of processes
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is
\mathbb{F}
-weakly tight, i.e. for any
{\varphi}\in\mathbb{F}
, the sequence
\Big(({\varphi}(u^{N}(t)))_{t\geq 0}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight in
\mathscr{D}\left([0,\infty);\mathbb{R}\right)
.

We start by proving Proposition
5.2
. A challenge for proving this result is the fact that the number of particles per deme is not bounded
a priori
. In order to average the process over compact spatial domains, we analyse the inner product of the approximate population densities with functions
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, i.e. with twice differentiable functions with compact support. Recall that in (
4.1
), we let
U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}
denote the approximate population density of particles whose number of mutations belong to the set of indices
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
Analogously to the definition of
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\cdot)
in (
4.9
), for
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we define the map
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}:\mathcal{S}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by letting, for
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}_{k}(x)}{N}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh.

(5.1)

To simplify notation, for
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, define
J_{L_{N}}({\varphi}):\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by letting

(J_{L_{N}}({\varphi}))(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh\quad\forall\,x\in\mathbb{R}.

(5.2)

Recall from (
4.1
) and (
2.8
) that
{U}^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(T,\cdot)
is given by the linear interpolation of
(U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(T,x))_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}
. Therefore, we have for
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
T\geq 0
and
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, recalling the definition of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{N}
in (
4.6
),

g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(T))=\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(T,\cdot),J_{L_{N}}({\varphi})\right\rangle_{N}=\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(T,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(T,x){\varphi}(x)\,dx.

(5.3)

We will first verify that the map
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
belongs to the domain of the infinitesimal generator
\mathcal{L}^{N}
defined in (
2.6
). For this, it will be convenient to introduce the discrete gradient
\nabla_{L_{N}}
and the discrete Laplacian
\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}_{L_{N}}
for any
{\varphi}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
by letting

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\nabla_{L_{N}}{\varphi}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=L_{N}\left({\varphi}(x+L_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x)\right),\\
\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}_{L_{N}}{\varphi}(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=L_{N}^{2}\left({\varphi}(x+L_{N}^{-1})+{\varphi}(x-L_{N}^{-1})-2{\varphi}(x)\right).\end{array}\right.

(5.4)

(Recall the analogous definition of
\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}
in (
4.11
)).
We highlight that the discrete gradient and the discrete Laplacian are defined in (
5.4
) for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, not only for
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
. Recall the definition of the reaction term
F=(F_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
given in (
2.12
), and the modified reaction term
F^{+}=(F^{+}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
given in (
4.10
), and the definition of
\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
in (
2.5
).

Lemma 5.4
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the map
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}:\mathcal{S}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
given in (
5.1
) satisfies the following conditions:

(i)

g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
.

(ii)

The map
\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\mapsto\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is in
\mathscr{C}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
, and is given by, for
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{m_{N}}{2L_{N}^{3}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\left(J_{L_{N}}(\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}_{L_{N}}{\varphi})\right)(x)\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}_{k}(x)}{N}

(5.5)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\left(J_{L_{N}}({\varphi})\right)(x)\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F_{k}\left(\frac{{\xi}(x)}{N}\right).

(iii)

The map
\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\mapsto\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is in
\mathscr{C}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
, and is given by, for
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})

(5.6)

\displaystyle=2g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})

\displaystyle+\frac{m_{N}}{2NL_{N}^{4}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\left((J_{L_{N}}(\nabla_{L_{N}}{\varphi}))^{2}(x)+(J_{L_{N}}(\nabla_{L_{N}}{\varphi}))^{2}(x-L_{N}^{-1})\right)\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}_{k}(x)}{N}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NL_{N}^{2}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}(J_{L_{N}}({\varphi}))^{2}(x)\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}\left(\frac{{\xi}(x)}{N}\right).

(iv)

For any
T\geq 0
and
p\geq 1
, the following estimate holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t))+\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t))+\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big]<\infty.

(5.7)

(v)

The process
(M(T))_{T\geq 0}
given by, for
T\geq 0
,

M(T)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Big(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(T))\Big)^{2}-\Big(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(0))\Big)^{2}-\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t-))\,dt

is a càdlàg martingale with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
.

Similarly to the proof of Lemma
4.2
, the proof of Lemma
5.4
follows from standard arguments, which we postpone to Section
0.A.6
in the appendix. We now use Lemma
5.4
to write
\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(T,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle
as a semimartingale. Recall the definition of
J_{L_{N}}({\varphi})
in (
5.2
).

Lemma 5.5
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, there exist a càdlàg square integrable martingale
M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
with
M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(0)=0
, and a finite variation process
A^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
such that for
T\geq 0
,

\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(T,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle=\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(0,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle+M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(T)+A^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(T),

where for
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle A^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(T)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\left(J_{L_{N}}(\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}_{L_{N}}{\varphi})\right)(x)\int_{0}^{T}\frac{m_{N}}{2L_{N}^{2}}U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x)\;dt

(5.8)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\left(J_{L_{N}}({\varphi})\right)(x)\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{0}^{T}F_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\;dt,

and the predictable bracket process of
M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
is given by, for
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left\langle M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\right\rangle(T)

(5.9)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{N}}{2NL_{N}^{4}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\left(\left(J_{L_{N}}(\nabla_{L_{N}}{\varphi})\right)^{2}(x)+\left(J_{L_{N}}(\nabla_{L_{N}}{\varphi})\right)^{2}(x-L_{N}^{-1})\right)\int_{0}^{T}U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(t-,x)\,dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NL_{N}^{2}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\left(J_{L_{N}}({\varphi})\right)^{2}(x)\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\int_{0}^{T}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\,dt.

Proof.

The result will follow from Lemma
5.4
and an application of Lemmas
0.A.1
and
0.A.2
in the appendix, and Theorem
3.2
. To see this, we first observe that combining Lemma
5.4
(i) with Theorem
3.2
, and then using (
5.3
), we conclude that for any
\mathcal{I}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, the process
(M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(t))_{t\geq 0}
given by, for
T\geq 0

\displaystyle M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(T)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(T,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle-\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathcal{I}}(0,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle-\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t-))\,dt

(5.10)

is a càdlàg martingale with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{{\eta}}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
. Then, defining
(A^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(t))_{t\geq 0}
by, for
T\geq 0
,

A^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(T)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t-))\,dt,

Lemma
5.4
(ii) and (
5.10
) imply (
5.8
).

It remains to compute the predictable bracket process of
M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
. Note that since the map
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}:\mathcal{S}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
does not depend on time, we have

\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\equiv 0\quad\forall\,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}.

(5.11)

We claim that
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
satisfies the conditions of Lemmas
0.A.1
and
0.A.2
in the appendix. Indeed, checking the conditions of Lemma
0.A.1
, conditions (i) and (iv) follow from Lemma
5.4
(iv) and (
5.11
), condition (ii) follows from (
5.11
), and condition (iii) follows from Lemma
5.4
(i) and (ii). Checking the conditions of Lemma
0.A.2
, condition (i) follows from Lemma
5.4
(iii), and condition (ii) follows from Lemma
5.4
(iv) and (
5.11
). Therefore, Lemma
0.A.2
implies that
M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
is square integrable, and for
T\geq 0
,

\left\langle M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\right\rangle(T)=\int_{0}^{T}\left(\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t-))-2g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(t-))\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t-))\right)\,dt.

(5.12)

Hence, applying (
5.6
) to (
5.12
), we get (
5.9
), which completes the proof.
∎

We now use Lemma
5.5
to derive a uniform in time bound (over a compact time interval) on the total mass of particles in a compact interval.

Lemma 5.6
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for any
R>0
and
T\geq 0
, there exists
C_{R,T}>0
such that

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\,\int_{-R}^{R}\|u^{N}(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\,dx\right]\leq C_{R,T}.

Proof.

Fix
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
such that
{\varphi}(x)\geq 0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
{\varphi}(x)\geq 1
for all
x\in[-R,R]
. Then recalling from (
4.1
) that for
t\in[0,T]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
we have
U^{N}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(t,x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}u^{N}_{k}(t,x)=\|u^{N}(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}
, and recalling (
5.3
), we have that almost surely for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{-R}^{R}\|u^{N}(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\,dx\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(t,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle.

(5.13)

By Lemma
5.5
, we can write

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(t,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle\leq\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(0,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(t)+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}A^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(t).

(5.14)

We will bound each term on the right-hand side of (
5.14
) separately. For the first term, we recall that, by Assumption
4
, the function
f=\left(f_{k}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
that determines the initial condition
\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}
in (
2.1
) satisfies
f\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\,{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, and so

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\left\langle U^{N}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(0,\cdot),{\varphi}\right\rangle\right]=\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}}U^{N}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(0,x){\varphi}(x)\,dx\right]\leq\|f\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{1}(\mathbb{R})}.

(5.15)

To bound the finite variation term on the right-hand side of (
5.14
), we observe that since
{\varphi}
is smooth, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, (
5.4
) yields

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}_{L_{N}}{\varphi}(x)

\displaystyle=L_{N}^{2}\Big(({\varphi}(x+L_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x))-({\varphi}(x)-{\varphi}(x-L_{N}^{-1}))\Big)

\displaystyle=L_{N}^{2}\int_{x}^{x+L_{N}^{-1}}({\varphi}^{\prime}(y)-{\varphi}^{\prime}(y-L_{N}^{-1}))\,dy

\displaystyle=L_{N}^{2}\int_{x}^{x+L_{N}^{-1}}\int_{y-L_{N}^{-1}}^{y}{\varphi}^{\prime\prime}(z)\,dz\,dy.

Therefore, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

|\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}_{L_{N}}{\varphi}(x)|\leq\|{\varphi}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

(5.16)

Recall from Assumption
1
(ii) that
L_{N}\rightarrow\infty
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Let
R_{{\varphi}}>0
be sufficiently large that
\supp({\varphi})\subset[-R_{{\varphi}}+2L_{N}^{-1},R_{{\varphi}}-2L_{N}^{-1}]
for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, applying (
5.16
) to (
5.8
), recalling (
5.2
) and using that by (
4.10
) and (
2.12
), the reaction term
F^{+}=(F^{+}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
satisfies
F^{+}_{k}(u)\geq 0\vee F_{k}(u)
for all
u\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have that for
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}A^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(t)\right]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L_{N}}\|{\varphi}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{{\varphi}}]}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{m_{N}}{2L_{N}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}^{N}}}\left[\|u^{N}(t-,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\right]\,dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L_{N}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{{\varphi}}]}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\right]\,dt.

Hence, by Theorem
3.3
and (
4.32
) in the proof of Lemma
4.4
, and using Assumption
1
(i)
, we conclude that there exists
C^{(1)}_{R,T,{\varphi}}>0
such that

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}A^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(t)\right]\leq C^{(1)}_{R,T,{\varphi}}.

(5.17)

Now, to bound the supremum of the martingale term on the right-hand side of (
5.14
), we first observe that by the smoothness of
{\varphi}
and by (
5.4
), we have for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\nabla_{L_{N}}{\varphi}(x)=L_{N}\Big({\varphi}(x+L_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x)\Big)=L_{N}\int_{x}^{x+L_{N}^{-1}}{\varphi}^{\prime}(y)\,dy,

and so for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

|\nabla_{L_{N}}{\varphi}(x)|\leq\|{\varphi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}.

(5.18)

Taking expectations on both sides of (
5.9
) and applying (
5.18
) and (
5.2
), we conclude that for
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\left\langle M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\right\rangle(T)\right]\leq

\displaystyle\frac{m_{N}}{NL_{N}^{4}}\|{\varphi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{{\varphi}}]}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\|u^{N}(t-,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\right]\,dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NL_{N}^{2}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{{\varphi}}]}\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\right]\,dt.

Hence, by Assumption
1
, (
4.32
) and Theorem
3.3
, and then by using Jensen’s inequality and the BDG inequality as in (
4.37
), we conclude that there exists
C^{(2)}_{R,T,{\varphi}}>0
such that

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|M^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}}(t)\right|\right]\leq C^{(2)}_{R,T,{\varphi}}.

(5.19)

Therefore, substituting estimates (
5.15
), (
5.17
) and (
5.19
) into (
5.14
), the result follows from (
5.13
).
∎

Recall from the end of Section
1
that we equip
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
with the vague topology. By combining Lemma
5.6
and the characterisation of compact subsets of
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
in the vague topology, we will now be able to prove Proposition
5.2
.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Let
\left(A_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}},\left(a_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,\infty)
be non-decreasing with
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}A_{n}=\infty
. Let
\mathcal{K}\subset\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
be the subset of Radon measures given by

\mathcal{K}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\left\{{\nu}\in\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R}):\,{\nu}([-A_{n},A_{n}])\leq a_{n}\;\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.

Then
\mathcal{K}
is a compact subset of
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
in the vague topology (see
[
16
, Section III.9 - Proposition 15]
). Furthermore, recalling (
2.10
), since
(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)
has the product topology, by Tychonoff’s theorem (see for instance
[
33
, Theorem 4.42]
), if
\left(\mathcal{K}_{k}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is a sequence of compact subsets of
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
, then the Cartesian product
\mathscr{K}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\prod_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\mathcal{K}_{k}
is a compact subset of
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
.

Take
T\geq 0
and for each
R\in\mathbb{N}
, define
C_{R,T}>0
as in Lemma
5.6
. For
{\varepsilon}\in(0,1)
, let
\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}=\prod_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\mathcal{K}_{k,{\varepsilon},T}
, where for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the set
\mathcal{K}_{k,{\varepsilon},T}
is given by

\mathcal{K}_{k,{\varepsilon},T}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\left\{{\nu}\in\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R}):{\nu}([-R,R])\leq\frac{2^{R}C_{R,T}}{{\varepsilon}}\,\forall\,R\in\mathbb{N}\right\}.

Then
\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}
is a compact subset of
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. Moreover, by the definition of
\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}
, and then, in the third line, by a union bound, Lemma
5.6
and Markov’s inequality, for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left(u^{N}(t)\in\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}\;\forall t\in[0,T]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{-R}^{R}u^{N}_{k}(t,x)\,dx\leq\frac{2^{R}C_{R,T}}{{\varepsilon}}\;\forall\,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\;\forall\,R\in\mathbb{N}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{-R}^{R}\|u^{N}(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\,dx\leq\frac{2^{R}C_{R,T}}{{\varepsilon}}\;\forall\,R\in\mathbb{N}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\sum_{R\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2^{R}}

\displaystyle=1-{\varepsilon},

which completes the proof, since
{\varepsilon}>0
was arbitrary.
∎

As explained at the start of this section, to conclude our proof of tightness for
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
, it will suffice to find a suitable set
\mathbb{F}\subset\mathscr{C}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}};\mathbb{R})
that separates points in
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
and that is closed under addition, and then prove that Proposition
5.3
holds with this choice of
\mathbb{F}
. We recall that, since
\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
is a dense subset of
\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
in the topology of uniform convergence (see for instance
[
29
, Exercises 3.10 and 3.11]
), it follows that
\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
separates the points of
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
(see for instance
[
16
, Proposition 2.9]
). Using this and the fact that
(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)
is equipped with the product topology (by (
2.10
)), it is immediate that the set

\displaystyle\mathbb{F}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Bigg\{{\varphi}:\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:

\displaystyle\exists\,n\in\mathbb{N}_{0},{\varphi}_{0},{\varphi}_{1},\ldots,{\varphi}_{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})\textrm{ such that}

(5.20)

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\varphi}(u)=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\left\langle u_{k},{\varphi}_{k}\right\rangle\quad\forall u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\Bigg\}

satisfies conditions (i) and (ii) of Proposition
5.3
. Then, applying the tightness criterion stated in
[
29
, Theorem 3.8.6]
and in
[
1
]
, in order to prove Proposition
5.3
, it will be enough to establish a weak compact containment condition and Aldous’ criterion. The weak compact containment condition reads as follows.

Lemma 5.7
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for
\mathbb{F}
as defined in (
5.20
), for any
{\varphi}\in\mathbb{F}
,
{\varepsilon}>0
and
T\geq 0
, there is a compact set
\mathscr{K}_{{\varphi},{\varepsilon},T}\subset\mathbb{R}
such that

\inf_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left({\varphi}(u^{N}(t))\in\mathscr{K}_{{\varphi},{\varepsilon},T}\;\;\forall t\in[0,T]\right)\geq 1-{\varepsilon}.

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{F}\subset\mathscr{C}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}};\mathbb{R})
, and by the fact that the continuous image of a compact set is compact, the result follows from Proposition
5.2
.
∎

We now proceed to establishing Aldous’ criterion
[
1
]
.

Lemma 5.8

(Aldous’ criterion)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for
\mathbb{F}
as defined in (
5.20
), the following holds. For
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
T\geq 0
, let
\mathscr{T}^{N}(T)
be the family of all
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
-stopping times
{\tau}
such that
{\tau}\leq T
almost surely. Then, for any
{\varphi}\in\mathbb{F}
,
{\varepsilon}>0
and
T\geq 0
,

\lim_{{\gamma}\downarrow 0}\;\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T),\\
0<{\theta}\leq{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left(\left|{\varphi}({u}^{N}(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T))-{\varphi}({u}^{N}({\tau}))\right|>{\varepsilon}\right)=0.

Proof.

By the definition of
\mathbb{F}
in (
5.20
), it will be enough to verify that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
,
{\varepsilon}>0
and
T\geq 0
,

\lim_{{\gamma}\downarrow 0}\;\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T),\\
0<{\theta}\leq{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left(\left|\left\langle{u}^{N}_{k}(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T,\cdot),{\phi}\right\rangle-\left\langle{u}^{N}_{k}({\tau},\cdot),{\phi}\right\rangle\right|>{\varepsilon}\right)=0.

(5.21)

To prove (
5.21
), we will apply the semimartingale formulation of
\left\langle u^{N}_{k}(T,\cdot),{\phi}\right\rangle
given by Lemma
5.5
. Recall from (
4.1
) that
u^{N}_{k}=U^{N}_{\{k\}}
. For any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T)
,
{\gamma}>0
and
{\theta}\in(0,{\gamma}]
, we have by Lemma
5.5
and the triangle inequality that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle{u}^{N}_{k}(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T,\cdot),{\phi}\right\rangle-\left\langle{u}^{N}_{k}({\tau},\cdot),{\phi}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T)-A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}({\tau})\right|+\left|M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T)-M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}({\tau})\right|.

Therefore, by Markov’s inequality, in order to prove (
5.21
), it will be enough to verify that

\lim_{{\gamma}\downarrow 0}\;\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T),\\
0<{\theta}\leq{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\left|A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}({\tau})\right|\right]=0,

(5.22)

and that

\lim_{{\gamma}\downarrow 0}\;\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T),\\
0<{\theta}\leq{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\left|M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}({\tau})\right|\right]=0.

(5.23)

We will start by proving (
5.22
). Let
R_{{\phi}}>0
be sufficiently large that
\supp({\phi})\subset[-R_{{\phi}}+2L_{N}^{-1},R_{{\phi}}-2L_{N}^{-1}]
for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that since by Assumption
1
,
L_{N}\rightarrow\infty
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, and since
{\phi}
has compact support, we can choose such an
R_{{\phi}}<\infty
. By the definition of
A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}
in (
5.8
) in the statement of Lemma
5.5
, and since
|F_{k}(u)|\leq F^{+}_{k}(u)
for
u\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
by (
2.12
) and (
4.10
), we have that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T)
,
{\gamma}>0
and
{\theta}\in(0,{\gamma}]
,

\displaystyle\left|A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}({\tau})\right|

(5.24)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}|\left(J_{L_{N}}(\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}_{L_{N}}{\phi})\right)(x)|\int_{{\tau}}^{({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T}\frac{m_{N}}{2L_{N}^{2}}u^{N}_{k}(t-,x)\;dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}|\left(J_{L_{N}}({\phi})\right)(x)|\int_{{\tau}}^{({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\;dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{N}\|{\phi}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}}{2L_{N}^{3}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\int_{0}^{T}u^{N}_{k}(t-,x)\cdot\mathds{1}_{\{{\tau}\leq t\leq{\tau}+{\theta}\}}\;dt

\displaystyle+\frac{\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\int_{0}^{T}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\cdot\mathds{1}_{\{{\tau}\leq t\leq{\tau}+{\theta}\}}\;dt,

where the second inequality follows by our choice of
R_{{\phi}}
and by (
5.16
) and (
5.2
). Since
{\theta}\in(0,{\gamma}]
, we have

\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{\{{\tau}\leq t\leq{\tau}+{\theta}\}}\;dt\right)^{1/2}=\left(\int_{{\tau}}^{({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T}\;dt\right)^{1/2}\leq\sqrt{{\theta}}\leq\sqrt{{\gamma}}.

(5.25)

Hence, applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and (
5.25
) to the integrals with respect to time on the right-hand side of (
5.24
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}({\tau})\right|

(5.26)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{N}\|{\phi}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{{\gamma}}}{2L_{N}^{3}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}u^{N}_{k}(t-,x)^{2}\;dt\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{{\gamma}}}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\left(F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\right)^{2}\;dt\Bigg)^{1/2}.

Therefore, taking expectations on both sides of (
5.26
), applying Fubini’s theorem, and then Jensen’s inequality and again Fubini’s theorem, we conclude that there exists
C_{q_{+},q_{-},f}(T)>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T)
,
{\gamma}>0
, and
{\theta}\in(0,{\gamma}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\left|A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-A^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}({\tau})\right|\right]

(5.27)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{N}\|{\phi}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{{\gamma}}}{2L_{N}^{3}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[u^{N}_{k}(t-,x)^{2}\right]\;dt\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sqrt{{\gamma}}}{L_{N}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\left(F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t,x))\right)^{2}\Big]\;dt\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{q_{+},q_{-},f}(T)\left(\frac{m_{N}\|{\phi}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}R_{{\phi}}}{L_{N}^{2}}+\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}R_{{\phi}}\right)\sqrt{{\gamma}},

where in the last inequality we used Theorem
3.3
, the definition of
F^{+}_{k}
in (
4.10
), the fact that
s_{j}\leq 1\;\forall j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by Assumption
2
(i) and (iii), and the fact that
q_{+}
and
q_{-}
are polynomials by Assumption
3
. Since by Assumption
1
(i)
, we have
\displaystyle\frac{m_{N}}{L_{N}^{2}}\rightarrow m\in(0,\infty)
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, (
5.27
) implies that (
5.22
) holds.

It remains to establish the limit in (
5.23
). Since, by Lemma
5.5
,
M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}
is a càdlàg martingale with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
, by the optional sampling theorem (see for instance
[
29
, Theorem 2.2.13]
), for any
{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T)
,
(M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}({\tau}+t))_{t\geq 0}
is a càdlàg martingale with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{({\tau}+t)+}\}_{t\geq 0}
. Then, recalling the expression for the predictable bracket process of
M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}
in (
5.9
) in the statement of Lemma
5.5
, by (
5.18
), (
5.2
) and the fact that we chose
R_{\phi}
such that
\supp({\phi})\subset[-R_{\phi}+2L_{N}^{-1},R_{\phi}-2L_{N}^{-1}]\;\forall\,N\in\mathbb{N}
, for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T)
,
{\gamma}>0
and
{\theta}\in(0,{\gamma}]
,

\displaystyle\left\langle M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\right\rangle\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-\left\langle M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\right\rangle({\tau})

(5.28)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{N}\|{\phi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}}{NL_{N}^{4}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\int_{0}^{T}u^{N}_{k}(t-,x)\cdot\mathds{1}_{\{{\tau}\leq t\leq{\tau}+{\theta}\}}\;dt

\displaystyle+\frac{\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}}{NL_{N}^{2}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\int_{0}^{T}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\cdot\mathds{1}_{\{{\tau}\leq t\leq{\tau}+{\theta}\}}\;dt.

By an argument analogous to the one we used to derive estimate (
5.26
), applying (
5.25
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to (
5.28
), it follows that

\displaystyle\left\langle M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\right\rangle\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-\left\langle M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\right\rangle({\tau})

(5.29)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{N}\|{\phi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sqrt{{\gamma}}}{NL_{N}^{4}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}u^{N}_{k}(t-,x)^{2}\;dt\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sqrt{{\gamma}}}{NL_{N}^{2}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\left(F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\right)^{2}\,dt\Bigg)^{1/2}.

Then, taking expectations on both sides of (
5.29
) and applying Fubini’s theorem, and then Jensen’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem, we conclude that there exists
C^{\prime}_{q_{+},q_{-},f}(T)>0
such that for any
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T)
,
{\gamma}>0
and
{\theta}\in(0,{\gamma}]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\left\langle M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\right\rangle\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-\left\langle M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\right\rangle({\tau})\right]

(5.30)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m_{N}\|{\phi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sqrt{{\gamma}}}{NL_{N}^{4}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[u^{N}_{k}(t-,x)^{2}\right]\;dt\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sqrt{{\gamma}}}{NL_{N}^{2}}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}\cap[-R_{{\phi}},R_{{\phi}}]}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\left(F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t-,x))\right)^{2}\Big]\;dt\Bigg)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{q_{+},q_{-},f}(T)\left(\frac{m_{N}\|{\phi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}R_{{\phi}}}{NL_{N}^{3}}+\frac{\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}R_{{\phi}}}{NL_{N}}\right)\sqrt{{\gamma}},

where in the last inequality we used Theorem
3.3
, the definition of
F^{+}_{k}
in (
4.10
), the fact that
s_{j}\leq 1\;\forall j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by Assumption
2
(i) and (iii), and the fact that
q_{+}
and
q_{-}
are polynomials by Assumption
3
, in the same way as for (
5.27
). Finally, we conclude from Assumption
1
and (
5.30
) that

\lim_{{\gamma}\downarrow 0}\;\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\tau}\in\mathscr{T}^{N}(T),\\
0<{\theta}\leq{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\left\langle M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\right\rangle\Big(({\tau}+{\theta})\wedge T\Big)-\left\langle M^{N,{\phi}}_{\{k\}}\right\rangle({\tau})\right]=0,

which implies (
5.23
) by an application of Jensen’s inequality and the BDG inequality as in (
4.37
). Hence, both (
5.22
) and (
5.23
) hold, and therefore, as explained after (
5.21
), this establishes (
5.21
) and completes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
5.3
.

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

We checked before (
5.20
) that
\mathbb{F}
satisfies conditions (i) and (ii). By the tightness criterion stated in
[
29
, Theorem 3.8.6]
and in
[
1
]
, the result follows directly from Lemmas
5.7
and
5.8
.
∎

We can finally prove Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

By Jakubowski’s tightness criterion
[
44
, Theorem 3.1]
, since the metric space
(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)
is complete and separable (see e.g. the comment before Proposition 3.4.6 in
[
29
]
for a discussion on the product topology), the result follows directly from Propositions
5.2
and
5.3
.
∎

6
Characterisation of the limiting process

Proposition
5.1
implies the weak convergence of subsequences of
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
to a limiting process
u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in
\mathscr{D}\left([0,\infty),(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)
for any
T>0
. To complete the proof of Theorem
2.1
, it remains to establish that any subsequential limit is a solution to the system of PDEs (
2.11
), and then to prove the uniqueness of the limit. A major challenge to overcome is that we must verify that there exists a sequence of measurable functions
v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
such that for each
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
v_{k}
is the density of the measure
u_{k}
with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
, and then that the sequence of densities
v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
satisfy the system of PDEs (
2.11
).

To achieve this goal, it will be useful to establish tightness of
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
in a functional space, so that the sequence of densities
v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
coincides with the subsequential limit of
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
in this functional space. Bearing this aim in mind, recall from Section
1
that we let
{\lambda}
denote the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and now, for any
T\geq 0
, let
\hat{{\lambda}}
denote the measure on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
given by

\hat{{\lambda}}(dt\;dx)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{\mathds{1}_{\{t\in[0,T]\}}}{1+|x|^{2}}{\lambda}(dt\;dx).

(6.1)

For
T>0
and
r\in[1,\infty)
, we introduce the functional space

\displaystyle{\displaystyle L}_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Bigg\{v:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}\;\textrm{s.t.}\;\|v\|_{{L}_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\|v(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{r}}{1+|x|^{2}}\,dx\,dt\Bigg)^{1/r}<\infty\Bigg\}.

Remark 6.1
.

Observe that, by (
6.2
), the space
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
differs from the space

{L}_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Bigg\{v:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\;\textrm{s.t.}\;\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\,\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|v_{k}(t,x)|^{r}}{1+|x|^{2}}\,dx\,dt<\infty\Bigg\}.

In particular, the advantage of embedding the sequence of density processes in
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
rather than in
{L}_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{N}_{0},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})
is because of the structure of the reaction term
F=(F_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
defined in (
2.12
), since for
u\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the expression defining
F_{k}(u)
contains polynomials of
\|u\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}
.

Since
{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
is a complete and separable Banach space, for every
r\in[1,\infty)
,
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
is also a complete and separable Banach space when equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}
defined in (
6.2
) (see
[
43
, Proposition 1.2.29]
for a proof of this fact). Recall that under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
,
q_{-}
is a polynomial satisfying Assumption
3
, and, by Theorem
3.3
and since
u^{N}_{k}(t,x)
is defined by linear interpolation for
x\not\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
, for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
T\geq 0
, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\|u^{N}(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}{1}}^{4\deg q_{-}}\Big]}{1+|x|^{2}}\,dx\,dt<\infty,

which implies that
(u^{N}(t,x))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}
is almost surely an element of
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
. We can then study the tightness of the sequence
((u^{N}(t,x))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
in
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, and use it to characterise any limiting
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
-valued process
u
. Recall the definition of
\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
from Section
1
.
For
{\rho}\in\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
and
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, let
\langle{\rho},{\varphi}\rangle
be defined as in Section
1
. Recall that the measure defined in (
2.9
) induced by the random function
u^{N}_{k}(t,\cdot)
is denoted by
u^{N}_{k}(t)
, for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
t\geq 0
. Recall the definition of a mild solution to the system of PDEs (
2.11
) in Definition
3.5
. Recall from Section
1
that
for
H\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
[\![H]\!]\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\{1,2,\ldots,H\}
. We are now ready to state the main result of this section.

Proposition 6.2
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for any
T>0
, the sequence

\Big((u^{N}(t))_{t\in[0,T]},(u^{N}(t,x))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

is tight in
\mathscr{D}\left([0,T],(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)\times L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
. Moreover, the following conditions are satisfied by any subsequential limit
\Big((u(t))_{t\in[0,T]},\,(v(t,x))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)
:

(i)

For every
H\in\mathbb{N}
,
(t_{h})_{h\in[\![H]\!]}\in[0,T)^{H}
,
({\varphi}_{h})_{h\in[\![H]\!]}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})^{H}
and
(k_{h})_{h\in[\![H]\!]}\in(\mathbb{N}_{0})^{H}
,

(\langle u_{k_{h}}(t_{h}),\,{\varphi}_{h}\rangle)_{h\in[\![H]\!]}\overset{d}{{=}}\Bigg(\lim_{t^{\prime}\downarrow t_{h}}\frac{1}{t^{\prime}-t_{h}}\int_{t_{h}}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}v_{k_{h}}({\tau},x){\varphi}_{h}(x)\,dx\,d{\tau}\Bigg)_{h\in[\![H]\!]}.

(6.3)

(ii)

(v(t,x))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}
is a mild solution to the system of PDEs (
2.11
).

For a subsequential limit
(u,v)\in\mathscr{D}\left([0,T],(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)\times L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
as in Proposition
6.2
, we will refer to the random function
v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
as the limiting density process. In the remainder of this section, we will prove Proposition
6.2
. For didactic reasons, we split the proof into two subsections. We will establish the desired tightness in Section
6.1
, and then characterise the limiting processes
\Big((u(t))_{t\in[0,T]},\,(v(t,x))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)
in Section
6.2
.

6.1
Tightness in
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})

Since Proposition
5.1
provides the tightness of
((u^{N}(t))_{t\in[0,T]})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
in
\mathscr{D}\left([0,\infty),(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)
, it will suffice to establish that
((u^{N}(t,x))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight in
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
. Our first step in this direction will be to establish conditions that guarantee the desired tightness. These conditions rely on the characterisation of compact subsets of
{L}_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
given by Díaz and Mayoral’s compactness theorem
[
22
, Theorem 3.2]
. It will be convenient to introduce some more notation.
For
T>0
and
r\in[1,\infty)
, define the functional space

\displaystyle L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Bigg\{v^{*}:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\;\textrm{s.t.}\;\|v^{*}\|_{L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{|v^{*}(t,x)|^{r}}{1+|x|^{2}}\,dx\,dt\Bigg)^{1/r}<\infty\Bigg\},

and observe that
\Big(L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R}),\|\cdot\|_{L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\Big)
is also a complete and separable Banach space by the usual theory of
L_{r}
spaces (see e.g.
[
33
, Section 6.1]
). We also define, for any
{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}
, the following shift maps: for any function
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, we let

({\theta}^{(1)}_{{\gamma}}g)(t,x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=g(t+{\gamma},x)\mathds{1}_{\{t+{\gamma}\in[0,T]\}}\quad\textrm{and}\quad({\theta}^{(2)}_{{\gamma}}g)(t,x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=g(t,x+{\gamma})\quad\forall\,(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}.

(6.5)

We now state a criterion for relative compactness in
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, which will be used for a tightness criterion.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
T>0
and
r\in[1,\infty)
be fixed. Let
C_{1},C_{2}>0
,
{\beta}>1
,
J\in\mathbb{N}
, let
(k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
be strictly increasing, and let
(D_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,\infty)
be such that
D_{h}\rightarrow 0
as
h\rightarrow\infty
. Define the following subsets of
L\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{(1)}(C_{1},T)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Big\{v\in L:\;\|v\|_{{L}_{2r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}\leq C_{1}\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{(2)}((k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(D_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},T)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Bigg\{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in L:\;\sum_{k=k_{h}}^{\infty}\|v_{k}\|_{{L}_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}<D_{h}\;\forall\,h\in\mathbb{N}\Bigg\},

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}_{(3)}(C_{2},{\beta},J,T)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Big\{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in L:\;\|{\theta}^{(i)}_{(-1)^{i^{\prime}}2^{-(k+j)}}v_{k}-v_{k}\|_{{L}_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\leq C_{2}{\beta}^{-(k+j)}

\displaystyle\forall\,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,j\in\mathbb{N}\cap[J,\infty),\,i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,2\}\Big\},

and let
\mathscr{K}=\mathscr{K}(C_{1},C_{2},(k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(D_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},{\beta},J,T)\subset L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
denote the set

\mathscr{K}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathscr{K}_{(1)}(C_{1},T)\cap\mathscr{K}_{(2)}((k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(D_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},T)\cap\mathscr{K}_{(3)}(C_{2},{\beta},J,T).

Then
\mathscr{K}
is a relatively compact subset of
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
.

The proof of Lemma
6.3
relies on Díaz and Mayoral’s compactness theorem (see Theorem
0.A.8
in the appendix) and on the Kolmogorov-Riesz-Fréchet compactness theorem for the usual
L_{r}
spaces (see Theorem
0.A.9
in the appendix), and it is postponed until Section
0.A.4
in the appendix. The advantage of using Lemma
6.3
to prove tightness of a sequence of density processes is that it allows us to define relatively compact subsets of
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
as countable intersections of certain sets. Our next result uses this strategy to give a tightness criterion.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
T>0
and
r\in[1,\infty)
be fixed, and let
((v^{N}_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
-valued random variables such that the following conditions hold:

(i)

The
2r
-moments of
\Big((v^{N}(t,x))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
are uniformly bounded, i.e.

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\|v^{N}(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{2r}\Big]<\infty.

(ii)

The following limit holds:

\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\;\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{j=k}^{\infty}\,|v^{N}_{j}(t,x)|\Bigg]=0.

(iii)

There exist
l_{1},l_{2},C_{T}>0
such that for every
{\gamma}\in(-1,1)
and
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|{\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v^{N}_{k}-v^{N}_{k}\|_{{L}_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\Big]\leq C_{T}|{\gamma}|^{l_{i}}.

Then
((v^{N}_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is tight in
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
.

Remark 6.5
.

We collect here some observations regarding Lemma
6.4
.

(a)

Although condition (iii) is stated for arbitrary
l_{1},l_{2}>0
, in applications we typically consider
l_{1},l_{2}\in(0,1)
, since the case
l_{1},l_{2}>1
allows one to apply the Kolmogorov continuity criterion and derive a stronger result than that stated in Lemma
6.4
.

(b)

Lemma
6.4
applies directly to finite-dimensional stochastic processes, since we can identify
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with a subspace of
{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
via the canonical embedding
\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto(x_{1},\dots,x_{d},0,0,\dots)
.

Proof of Lemma
6.4
.

We must establish that for every
{\varepsilon}>0
, there exists a compact set
\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}\subset L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
such that

\inf_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{P}\Big((v_{k}^{N}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\in\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}\Big)\geq 1-{\varepsilon}.

(6.6)

It will suffice to construct
\mathscr{K}_{{\varepsilon},T}
satisfying both (
6.6
) and the conditions of Lemma
6.3
. First, we observe that by (
6.2
) and Jensen’s inequality, and then by Fubini’s theorem and condition (i), there exists
C_{1}=C_{1}(T)>0
such that

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\|v^{N}\|_{L_{2r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}\Big]\leq\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\|v^{N}(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{2r}}{1+|x|^{2}}\,dx\,dt\Bigg]^{1/(2r)}\leq C_{1}.

Then, using the notation introduced in the statement of Lemma
6.3
, we have by Markov’s inequality,

\inf_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{N}\in\mathscr{K}_{(1)}(4C_{1}{\varepsilon}^{-1},T)\Big)\geq 1-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4}.

(6.7)

Moreover, by using condition (ii), Fubini’s theorem, (
6.4
) and the fact that the measure
\hat{{\lambda}}
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
defined in (
6.1
) is finite, we conclude that there exists a strictly increasing sequence
(k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}}=(k_{h}({\varepsilon}))_{h\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that for every
h\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k=k_{h}}^{\infty}\|v^{N}_{k}\|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\Bigg]\leq\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2^{2h+2}}.

Recall the definition of
\mathscr{K}_{(2)}((k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(2^{-h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},T)
from the statement of Lemma
6.3
. By Markov’s inequality and a union bound, we have

\inf_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{N}\in\mathscr{K}_{(2)}((k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(2^{-h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},T)\Big)\geq 1-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{4}.

(6.8)

In the language of Lemma
6.3
, it remains to determine
C_{2}({\varepsilon},T)>0
,
{\beta}({\varepsilon},T)>1
and
J({\varepsilon},T)\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\inf_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{N}\in\mathscr{K}_{(3)}(C_{2},{\beta},J,T)\Big)\geq 1-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{2}.

(6.9)

Let
l=l_{1}\wedge l_{2}>0
, and take
{\beta}\in(1,2^{l})
. For
i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,2\}
,
N,j\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, define the event

\mathscr{A}^{i^{\prime},N}_{i,k,j,{\beta}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Big\{\|{\theta}^{(i)}_{(-1)^{i^{\prime}}2^{-(k+j)}}v_{k}^{N}-v_{k}^{N}\|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\geq C_{T}{\beta}^{-(k+j)}\Big\},

where
C_{T}>0
is the constant introduced in condition (iii). Then, by condition (iii) and Markov’s inequality, we have

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\,\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathscr{A}^{i^{\prime},N}_{i,k,j,{\beta}}\Big)\leq\left(\frac{{\beta}}{2^{l}}\right)^{k+j}.

Hence, since by construction we have
{\beta}<2^{l}
, by a union bound, we have for every
J\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\,\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\bigcup_{i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,2\}}\,\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\,\bigcup_{j\geq J}\mathscr{A}^{i^{\prime},N}_{i,k,j,{\beta}}\Bigg)\leq 4\left(\frac{2^{l}}{2^{l}-{\beta}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{{\beta}}{2^{l}}\right)^{J}.

(6.10)

Since by construction we have
{\beta}\in(1,2^{l})
, the right-hand side of (
6.10
) vanishes as
J\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, by taking
C_{2}=C_{T}
,
{\beta}\in(1,2^{l})
and then
J=J({\varepsilon},T)\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, we obtain (
6.9
). Finally, by combining (
6.7
), (
6.8
) and (
6.9
), we conclude that for every
{\varepsilon}>0
, there exist
C_{1},C_{2}>0
,
{\beta}>1
,
J\in\mathbb{N}
and a strictly increasing sequence
(k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that letting

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}(C_{1},C_{2},(k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(2^{-h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},{\beta},J,T)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\mathscr{K}_{(1)}(C_{1},T)\cap\mathscr{K}_{(2)}((k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(2^{-h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},T)\cap\mathscr{K}_{(3)}(C_{2},{\beta},J,T),

we have

\inf_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{P}\Big(v^{N}\in\mathscr{K}(C_{1},C_{2},(k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(2^{-h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},{\beta},J,T)\Big)\geq 1-{\varepsilon}.

Since Lemma
6.3
implies that
\mathscr{K}(C_{1},C_{2},(k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(2^{-h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},{\beta},J,T)
is relatively compact, (
6.6
) holds, which completes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove tightness of
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
in the space
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for any
T>0
, the sequence

\Big((u^{N}(t))_{t\in[0,T]},(u^{N}_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

is tight in
\mathscr{D}\left([0,T],(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)\times L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
.

Proof.

As mentioned at the beginning of Section
6.1
, by Proposition
5.1
, it will suffice to establish tightness in
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
. This follows if the sequence

(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}=\Big((u^{N}_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},t\in[0,T],x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

satisfies the conditions of Lemma
6.4
. Observe that
(u^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfies condition (i) by Theorem
3.3
(and since
u^{N}(t,x)
for
x\not\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
is defined by linear interpolation), condition (ii) by (
4.1
) and Lemma
4.7
, and condition (iii) by (
4.1
), (
6.5
), (
6.4
) and Lemma
4.5
. Therefore, Lemma
6.4
implies the desired tightness, which completes the proof.
∎

6.2
Characterisation of the limiting density process

We now proceed to characterise the limiting density process. Recall the definition of
\left(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d\right)
introduced in (
2.10
), and the definition of
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
in (
6.2
) for
T>0
and
r\in[1,\infty)
. To simplify notation, we will, throughout this subsection, assume that the sequence

\Big((u^{N}_{k}(t))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T]},(u^{N}_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}

is a subsequence that converges weakly in

\mathscr{D}\left([0,T],(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)\times L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})

to the random maps

\Big((u_{k}(t))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T]},\,(v_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big).

(6.11)

Our next result, which is a direct consequence of Skorokhod’s representation theorem, the properties of the Skorokhod
J_{1}
-topology and the fundamental theorem of calculus, establishes a useful relation between
u
as an
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
-valued process, and
v
as an element of
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
. Recall the definition of
\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
from Section
1
.
For
{\rho}\in\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
and
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, let
\langle{\rho},{\varphi}\rangle
be defined as in Section
1
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, for
(u,v)
as defined in (
6.11
), for every
H\in\mathbb{N}
,
(t_{h})_{h\in[\![H]\!]}\in[0,T)^{H}
,
({\varphi}_{h})_{h\in[\![H]\!]}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})^{H}
and
(k_{h})_{h\in[\![H]\!]}\in(\mathbb{N}_{0})^{H}
, the following equality holds in distribution:

(\langle u_{k_{h}}(t_{h}),\,{\varphi}_{h}\rangle)_{h\in[\![H]\!]}\overset{d}{=}\Bigg(\lim_{t^{\prime}\downarrow t_{h}}\frac{1}{t^{\prime}-t_{h}}\int_{t_{h}}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}v_{k_{h}}({\tau},x){\varphi}_{h}(x)\,dx\,d{\tau}\Bigg)_{h\in[\![H]\!]}.

Proof.

From now on in the proof, whenever
{\rho}
is a

\mathscr{D}([0,T],\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R}))
-valued random variable on the
probability space
({\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}},\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
, where

\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
is endowed with the vague topology, for
{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}
, we write

{\rho}(t,{\omega})
for the value at
t\in[0,T]
of the sample path

{\rho}(\cdot,{\omega})
. Likewise, if
{\nu}
is an

L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
-valued random
variable, we fix for
{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}
a measurable representative of
{\nu}({\omega})
and write

{\nu}(t,x,{\omega})=({\nu}_{k}(t,x,{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
for its value at
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
.

By Lemma
6.6
and Skorokhod’s representation theorem, it is possible to construct the random maps

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\Big((u^{N}_{k}({\tau},{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T],\,{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}},(u^{N}_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R},{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}},\\
\Big((u_{k}({\tau},{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T],\,{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}},\,(v_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R},\,{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}}\Big)\end{array}\right.

on the same probability space
({\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}},\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
in such a way that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\Big((u^{N}_{k}({\tau},{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T]},(u^{N}_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)

(6.12)

\displaystyle=\Big((u_{k}({\tau},{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T]},\,(v_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)

in
\mathscr{D}\left([0,T],(\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}},d)\right)\times L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
. Recall that
d_{\textrm{vague}}
introduced after (
2.9
) is a metric that induces the vague topology on
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})
, and that the metric
d
in
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
defined in (
2.10
) induces the product topology on
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. In particular, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and every
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, the map
\mathscr{M}(\mathbb{R})^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\ni{\rho}=({\rho}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\mapsto\langle{\rho}_{k},{\varphi}\rangle

is continuous with respect to the topology induced by
d
. Hence, by standard results on the Skorokhod
J_{1}
-topology (see e.g.
[
29
, Exercise 3.13]
), (
6.12
) implies that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}
the following holds: for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, the sequence of real-valued càdlàg processes
\Big((\langle u^{N}_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),{\varphi}\rangle)_{{\tau}\in[0,T]}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
(\langle u_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),{\varphi}\rangle)_{{\tau}\in[0,T]}
in
\mathscr{D}([0,T],\mathbb{R})
as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

Recall from Section
1
that we let
{\lambda}
denote the Lebesgue measure on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\;\forall\,d\in\mathbb{N}
. Since convergence in the Skorokhod
J_{1}
-topology implies pointwise convergence at any continuity point of the limit (see e.g.
[
29
, Proposition 3.5.2]
), and since the set of discontinuities for any càdlàg path is countable (see e.g.
[
29
, Lemma 3.5.1]
), we conclude that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}
the following holds: for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
,

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\langle u^{N}_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),{\varphi}\rangle=\langle u_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),{\varphi}\rangle\quad\textrm{ for }{\lambda}\textrm{-almost every }{\tau}\in[0,T].

(6.13)

Moreover, the set

\mathscr{K}_{k,{\varphi}}({\omega})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Big\{\big(\langle u_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),{\varphi}\rangle\big)_{{\tau}\in[0,T]},\big(\langle u_{k}^{N}({\tau},{\omega}),{\varphi}\rangle\big)_{{\tau}\in[0,T]}:N\in\mathbb{N}\Big\}\subset\mathscr{D}([0,T],\mathbb{R})

is compact with respect to the Skorokhod
J_{1}
-topology, which implies (see e.g.
[
29
, Exercise 3.16]
) that there exists
C_{k,{\varphi},T}({\omega})>0
such that

\sup_{h\in\mathscr{K}_{k,{\varphi}}({\omega})}\;\sup_{{\tau}\in[0,T]}\;|h({\tau})|\leq C_{k,{\varphi},T}({\omega}).

(6.14)

Therefore, using (
6.13
), (
6.14
) and dominated convergence, we conclude that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}
, the following holds: for any
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
0\leq t\leq t^{\prime}\leq T
,

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}\langle u_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),{\varphi}\rangle\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}\langle u^{N}_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),{\varphi}\rangle\,d{\tau}

(6.15)

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}u^{N}_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}){\varphi}(x)\,dx\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}v_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}){\varphi}(x)\,dx\,d{\tau},

where for the third equality we used the fact that
(u^{N}_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}
converges in
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
to
(v_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,{\tau}\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}
by (
6.13
), and the fact that
{\varphi}
has compact support.

Since the map
[0,T]\ni{\tau}\mapsto\langle u^{N}_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),\,{\varphi}\rangle
is right-continuous, by applying the fundamental theorem of calculus to (
6.15
), we conclude that for
\mathbb{P}
-almost every
{\omega}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 10\relax}}}
, the following holds: for any
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
t\in[0,T)
,

\langle u_{k}(t,{\omega}),\,{\varphi}\rangle=\lim_{t^{\prime}\downarrow t}\,\frac{1}{t^{\prime}-t}\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}\langle u_{k}({\tau},{\omega}),\,{\varphi}\rangle\,d{\tau}=\lim_{t^{\prime}\downarrow t}\,\frac{1}{t^{\prime}-t}\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}v_{k}({\tau},x,{\omega}){\varphi}(x)\,dx\,d{\tau}.

(6.16)

By observing that (
6.16
) holds for any choice of
t\in[0,T)
,
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the proof is complete.
∎

By Lemmas
6.6
and
6.7
, to complete the proof of Proposition
6.2
, it remains to establish that
v
is a mild solution to the system of PDEs (
2.11
). This will follow from our next result. Recall that under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, the function
f=(f_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
determines the initial condition
\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}
in (
2.1
). Recall the reaction term
F=(F_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
defined in (
2.12
), and recall from before (
3.4
) that we let
\{P_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the semigroup corresponding to a Brownian motion on
\mathbb{R}
run at speed
m\in(0,\infty)
. We also recall the Green’s function representation of
u^{N}
given in Lemma
4.3
. Moreover, recall from after (
4.2
) that we let
\{P^{N}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the semigroup associated with a simple symmetric random walk
(X^{N}(t))_{t\geq 0}
on
L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
with total jump rate
m_{N}
, and recall the definition of the normalised transition density
p^{N}
of
X^{N}
in (
4.3
). Also, for
t>0
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, recall the definition of the Gaussian kernel
p(t,x)
in (
3.3
).

Lemma 6.8
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
2.1
, the
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
-valued random variable
v
defined in (
6.11
) satisfies the following identity:

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\left|v_{k}(t,x)-(P_{t}f_{k})(x)-\int_{0}^{t}\Big(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(v({\tau},\cdot))\Big)(x)\,d{\tau}\right|\,dx\,dt\Bigg]=0.

Proof.

By Fubini’s theorem, the lemma will be proved after establishing that for every
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\left|v_{k}(t,x)-(P_{t}f_{k})(x)-\int_{0}^{t}\Big(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(v({\tau},\cdot))\Big)(x)\,d{\tau}\right|\,dx\,dt\Bigg]=0.

(6.17)

By Skorokhod’s representation theorem, it is possible to construct the sequence of density processes
\Big((u^{N}_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(v_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}
on the same probability space in such a way that

(u^{N}_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow(v_{k}(t,x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}

(6.18)

almost surely in
L_{4\deg q_{-}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Since the measure
\hat{{\lambda}}
on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
, defined in (
6.1
), is finite, by (
6.2
) and Jensen’s inequality the limit in (
6.18
) also holds almost surely in
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, for every
r\in[1,4\deg q_{-}]
. In particular, since convergence of a sequence of scalar-valued functions in
{L}_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})
implies the existence of a subsequence which converges
{\lambda}
-almost everywhere in
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
(see e.g.
[
33
, Corollary 2.32]
), (
6.18
) implies that for every
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, almost surely there exists a subsequence
(N^{(k)}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
such that for
{\lambda}
-almost every
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
,

\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}u^{N^{(k)}_{j}}_{k}(t,x)=v_{k}(t,x).

(6.19)

Moreover, by the definition of the reaction term
F=(F_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in (
2.12
), by the fact that by Assumption
3
,
q_{+},q_{-}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
are polynomials with
0\leq\deg q_{+}<\deg q_{-}
, and by (
0.A.50
) in Lemma
0.A.11
in the appendix, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
, the following limit holds almost surely

\int_{0}^{t}\Big(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(v({\tau},\cdot))\Big)(x)\,d{\tau}=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\Big(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u^{N}({\tau},\cdot))\Big)(x)\,d{\tau}.

(6.20)

Combining (
6.20
) and (
6.19
), and then using Fatou’s lemma (up to a subsequence if necessary), we conclude that for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the term on the left-hand side of (
6.17
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\left|v_{k}(t,x)-(P_{t}f_{k})(x)-\int_{0}^{t}\Big(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(v({\tau},\cdot))\Big)(x)\,d{\tau}\right|\,dx\,dt\Bigg]

(6.21)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\,\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\left|u^{N}_{k}(t,x)-(P_{t}f_{k})(x)-\int_{0}^{t}\Big(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u^{N}({\tau},\cdot))\Big)(x)\,d{\tau}\right|\,dx\,dt\Bigg].

Therefore, (
6.17
) will be proved after establishing that the right-hand side of (
6.21
) vanishes.

Our strategy will be to use the Green’s function representation given in Lemma
4.3
to prove that the limit on the right-hand side of (
6.21
) is equal to
0
. Observe that for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
and any non-negative real-valued function
g\in{L}_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{g(t,x)}{1+|x|^{2}}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{L_{N}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{g(t,x+hL_{N}^{-1})}{1+|x+hL_{N}^{-1}|^{2}}\,dh\,dt

(6.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{L_{N}(1+|x|^{2})}\int_{0}^{1}g(t,x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh\,dt,

where for the last inequality we used the fact that by Assumption
1
,
L_{N}\rightarrow\infty
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover, recall that the function
u^{N}_{k}(t,\cdot)
was defined after (
2.8
) via a linear interpolation in space between points in
L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
, and so for
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
and
h\in[0,1]
we have

|u^{N}_{k}(t,x)-u^{N}_{k}(t,x+hL_{N}^{-1})|\leq|u^{N}_{k}(t,x)-u^{N}_{k}(t,x+L_{N}^{-1})|.

Hence, by (
6.22
) and the triangle inequality, for
