, we conclude that the second factor on the right-hand side of (
0.A.37
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=H+1}^{\infty}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r+1}dx\,dt\Bigg)^{(r-1)/r}

\displaystyle\leq(2^{r+1}3)^{(r-1)/r}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=H+1}^{\infty}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r+1}dx\,dt\Bigg)^{(r-1)/r}

\displaystyle\leq(2^{r+1}3)^{(r-1)/r}\|w\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}}^{(r+1)(r-1)/r}\|v\|_{{L}_{r+1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}^{(r+1)(r-1)/r}.

Since
r\geq 1
, we have
r+1\leq 2r
, and therefore, by (
0.A.26
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=H+1}^{\infty}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r+1}dx\,dt\Bigg)^{(r-1)/r}

(0.A.40)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{r,w,T}C^{(r+1)(r-1)/r}_{1}.

We now proceed to bound the first factor on the right-hand side of (
0.A.37
). Observe that by using (
0.A.39
) with
q=1
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\sum_{k=H+1}^{\infty}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\,dx\,dt\Bigg)^{1/r}

(0.A.41)

\displaystyle\leq 6^{1/r}\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\sum_{k=H+1}^{\infty}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)|\,dx\,dt\Bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq 6^{1/r}\|w\|^{1/r}_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}}\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=H+1}^{\infty}\|v_{k}\|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\Bigg)^{1/r}.

By the definition of
\mathscr{K}_{(2)}((k_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},(D_{h})_{h\in\mathbb{N}},T)
in the statement of the lemma, the right-hand side of (
0.A.41
) vanishes as
H\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore, for every
{\varepsilon}>0
, we can take
H\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large that

\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\sum_{k=H+1}^{\infty}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\,dx\,dt\Bigg)^{1/r}\lesssim_{r,w}{\varepsilon}.

(0.A.42)

Applying (
0.A.40
) and (
0.A.42
) to (
0.A.37
), we conclude that for
H\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, for any
{\gamma}\in(-1,1)
,

\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=H+1}^{\infty}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r}dx\,dt\lesssim_{r,w,T}C_{1}^{(r+1)(r-1)/r}{\varepsilon}.

(0.A.43)

We will now bound the integral (
0.A.35
). As in the derivation of (
0.A.37
), by applying Hölder’s inequality with exponents
r
and
r/(r-1)
in the case
r>1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r}\,dx\,dt

(0.A.44)

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\,dx\,dt\Bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\cdot\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r+1}dx\,dt\Bigg)^{(r-1)/r}.

We will bound each factor on the right-hand side of (
0.A.44
) separately. Starting with the second factor, as in the derivation of (
0.A.40
), by using (
0.A.39
), we have

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r+1}dx\,dt\Bigg)^{(r-1)/r}

\displaystyle\leq(2^{r+1}3)^{(r-1)/r}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r+1}dx\,dt\Bigg)^{(r-1)/r}

\displaystyle\leq(2^{r+1}3)^{(r-1)/r}\|w\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}}^{(r+1)(r-1)/r}\|v\|_{{L}_{r+1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}^{(r+1)(r-1)/r},

and therefore, by (
0.A.26
),

\displaystyle\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r+1}dx\,dt\Bigg)^{(r-1)/r}

(0.A.45)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{r,w,T}C_{1}^{(r+1)(r-1)/r}.

It remains to bound the first factor on the right-hand side of (
0.A.44
). Without loss of generality, assume
{\gamma}>0
, as the case
{\gamma}<0
can be treated analogously. We start by observing that the triangle inequality, and then a suitable substitution and (
0.A.38
) imply that for every
({\gamma}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\cap{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
with
{\gamma}_{0}=0
and
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}{\gamma}_{j}\leq 1
,
i\in\{1,2\}
,
A\in\mathbb{N}
and
g\in{L}_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Big|{\theta}^{(i)}_{\sum_{j=1}^{A}{\gamma}_{j}}g-g\Big|\Big|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{A}\Big|\Big|{\theta}^{(i)}_{\sum_{l=1}^{j}{\gamma}_{l}}g-{\theta}^{(i)}_{\sum_{l=0}^{j-1}{\gamma}_{l}}g\Big|\Big|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}

(0.A.46)

\displaystyle\leq 3\sum_{j=1}^{A}\|{\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}_{j}}g-g\|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}.

By taking the limit as
A\rightarrow\infty
on both sides of (
0.A.46
) and using Remark
0.A.10
, we conclude that for any
({\gamma}_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subset[0,1]^{\mathbb{N}_{0}}\cap{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
with
{\gamma}_{0}=0
and
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}{\gamma}_{j}\leq 1
,
i\in\{1,2\}
and
g\in{L}_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})
,

\Big|\Big|{\theta}^{(i)}_{\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}{\gamma}_{j}}g-g\Big|\Big|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\leq 3\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\|{\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}_{j}}g-g\|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}.

(0.A.47)

Recall the role of the parameter
J\in\mathbb{N}
in the definition of the set
\mathscr{K}_{(3)}(C_{2},{\beta},J,T)
in the statement of Lemma
6.3
. Take
J^{*}\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying
J^{*}\geq J
, whose exact value will be specified later. Recalling that
{\beta}>1
, suppose
H\in\mathbb{N}
is sufficiently large that
H+1<{\beta}^{H+1}
. By taking
{\gamma}\in(0,2^{-(H+J^{*})})
, we conclude from the dyadic expansion of
{\gamma}
that there exists
\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}\subseteq\mathbb{N}\cap[H+J^{*}+1,\infty)
such that
{\gamma}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}2^{-j}
. By applying this representation and (
0.A.47
), and then using the definition of
\mathscr{K}_{(3)}(C_{2},{\beta},J,T)
, we conclude that the first factor on the right-hand side of (
0.A.44
) is bounded, for all
v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}
, by

\displaystyle\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\,dx\,dt\Bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{1/r}\|w\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}}^{1/r}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}\,\sum_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}}\|{\theta}^{(i)}_{2^{-j}}v_{k}-v_{k}\|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\Bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{1/r}\|w\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}}^{1/r}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}\,\sum_{j=H+J^{*}+1}^{\infty}\|{\theta}^{(i)}_{2^{-j}}v_{k}-v_{k}\|_{L_{1}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})}\Bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{1/r}\|w\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}}^{1/r}C_{2}^{1/r}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}\,\sum_{j=H+J^{*}+1}^{\infty}{\beta}^{-j}\Bigg)^{1/r}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{3\|w\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}}{\beta}C_{2}(H+1)}{{\beta}^{H+J^{*}+1}({\beta}-1)}\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{3\|w\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}}{\beta}C_{2}}{{\beta}^{J^{*}}({\beta}-1)}\right)^{1/r},

where for the fourth line we used the fact that
{\beta}>1
, and the last inequality follows from our choice of
H
. Hence, using the same argument for the case
{\gamma}<0
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, we can choose
H,J^{*}\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large that for any
{\gamma}\in(-2^{-(H+J^{*})},2^{-(H+J^{*})})
,

\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\,dx\,dt\Bigg)^{1/r}\lesssim_{r,w,{\beta},C_{2}}{\varepsilon}.

(0.A.48)

By applying (
0.A.45
) and (
0.A.48
) to (
0.A.44
), we conclude that for
{\varepsilon}>0
, by choosing
H\in\mathbb{N}
and
J^{*}\geq J
sufficiently large, for any
{\gamma}\in(-2^{-(H+J^{*})},2^{-(H+J^{*})})
,

\sup_{v=(v_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in\mathscr{K}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|x|^{2}}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{H}|w_{k}v_{k}(t,x)-w_{k}({\theta}^{(i)}_{{\gamma}}v_{k})(t,x)|\Bigg)^{r}\,dx\,dt\lesssim_{r,w,T,C_{1},{\beta},C_{2}}{\varepsilon}.

(0.A.49)

Applying (
0.A.49
) and (
0.A.43
) to (
0.A.35
) and (
0.A.36
), we conclude that by choosing
H\in\mathbb{N}
and
J^{*}\geq J
sufficiently large, and then letting
{\gamma}\in(-2^{-(H+J^{*})},2^{-(H+J^{*})})
, we obtain (
0.A.33
). As explained before (
0.A.33
), it then follows by Theorem
0.A.9
that for every
w\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{\infty}
, the set
\mathscr{K}_{w}^{*}
is a relatively compact subset of
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};\mathbb{R})
, i.e. that
\mathscr{K}
is scalarly relatively compact.

Finally, combining steps
(1)
-
(4)
, we conclude the set
\mathscr{K}
satisfies the conditions of Theorem
0.A.8
, and therefore that
\mathscr{K}
is relatively compact in
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
.
∎

Next, we establish a result on
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
which is used in the proof of Lemma
6.8
in Section
6.2
.

Lemma 0.A.11
.

Suppose
m>0
, and let
p:(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow(0,\infty)
be defined as in (
3.3
). Let
T>0
and
r\in[4,\infty)
be fixed. Let

({\nu}^{N})_{N\in\mathbb{N}}=\Big(({\nu}^{N}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\Big)_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\subset L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})

denote a sequence that converges to
{\nu}=({\nu}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in
L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Then, for any
l\in\{0\}\cup[1,r/4]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, and
(t,x)\in\mathbb{[}0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-{\tau},x-y){\nu}^{N}_{k}({\tau},y)\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}\,dy\,d{\tau}

(0.A.50)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-{\tau},x-y){\nu}_{k}({\tau},y)\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}\,dy\,d{\tau}.

Proof.

It will suffice to show that for all
l\in\{0\}\cup[1,r/4]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
,

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-{\tau},x-y)\Big|{\nu}^{N}_{k}({\tau},y)\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}-{\nu}_{k}({\tau},y)\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}\,\Big|\,dy\,d{\tau}=0.

(0.A.51)

Observe that for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
l\in\{0\}\cup[1,r/4]
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
(t,y)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
, by the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\Big|{\nu}^{N}_{k}({\tau},y)\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}-{\nu}_{k}({\tau},y)\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}\,\Big|

(0.A.52)

\displaystyle\leq|{\nu}^{N}_{k}({\tau},y)-{\nu}_{k}({\tau},y)|\cdot\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}+|{\nu}^{N}_{k}({\tau},y)|\cdot\Big|\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}-\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}\Big|\

\displaystyle\leq\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)-{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\cdot\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}

\displaystyle+l\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big(\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l-1}+\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l-1}\Big)\Big|\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}-\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq(l+1)\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)-{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big(\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{l},

where the second inequality follows from the elementary inequality (
4.63
) and since
|{\nu}^{N}_{k}({\tau},y)|\leq\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}
, and the third inequality follows from the triangle inequality. We use (
0.A.52
) to bound the argument of the limit on the left-hand side of (
0.A.51
), obtaining

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-{\tau},x-y)\Big|{\nu}^{N}_{k}({\tau},y)\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}-{\nu}_{k}({\tau},y)\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{l}\,\Big|\,dy\,d{\tau}

(0.A.53)

\displaystyle\leq(l+1)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+|y|^{2})^{1/2}p(t-{\tau},x-y)

\displaystyle\cdot\left((1+|y|^{2})^{-1/2}\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)-{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big(\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{l}\right)\,dy\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq(l+1)\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+|y|^{2})p^{2}(t-{\tau},x-y)\,dy\,d{\tau}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|y|^{2}}\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)-{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{2}\Big(\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{2l}\,dy\,d{\tau}\right)^{1/2},

where for the third inequality we used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. By the definition of the Gaussian kernel
p
in (
3.3
), we have for all
(t,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+|y|^{2})p^{2}(t-{\tau},x-y)\,dy\,d{\tau}

(0.A.54)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{{\pi}m(t-{\tau})}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(1+|y|^{2})\frac{1}{\sqrt{{\pi}m(t-{\tau})}}\exp\left(-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{m(t-{\tau})}\right)\,dy\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{{\pi}m(t-{\tau})}}\left((1+x^{2})+\tfrac{m}{2}(t-{\tau})\right)\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle=(1+x^{2})\sqrt{\tfrac{t}{{\pi}m}}+\frac{1}{6}\sqrt{\tfrac{m}{{\pi}}}t^{3/2}.

Hence, by (
0.A.53
) and (
0.A.54
), the limit in (
0.A.51
) will be proved after establishing that the second factor on the right-hand side of (
0.A.53
) vanishes as
N\rightarrow\infty
. By applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the second factor on the right-hand side of (
0.A.53
) and the definition of
L_{4}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
in (
6.4
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|y|^{2}}\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)-{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{2}\Big(\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{2l}\,dy\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\nu}^{N}-{\nu}\|_{L_{4}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}^{2}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|y|^{2}}\Big(\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{4l}\,dy\,d{\tau}\right)^{1/2}.

By applying the elementary inequality (
4.68
), the definition of
L_{4l}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
in (
6.4
) for
l\in[1,r/4]
,, the elementary inequality
\sqrt{a+b}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}\;\forall\,a,b\geq 0
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{1+|y|^{2}}\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)-{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{2}\Big(\|{\nu}^{N}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}+\|{\nu}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big)^{2l}\,dy\,d{\tau}

(0.A.55)

\displaystyle\leq\|{\nu}^{N}-{\nu}\|_{L_{4}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}^{2}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\mathds{1}_{\{l=0\}}\hat{{\lambda}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})^{1/2}+\mathds{1}_{\{l\in[1,r/4]\}}2^{(4l-1)/2}\left(\|{\nu}^{N}\|_{L_{4l}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}^{2l}+\|{\nu}\|_{L_{4l}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}^{2l}\right)\right).

Observe that by Hölder’s inequality and the fact the measure
\hat{{\lambda}}
defined in (
6.1
) is a finite measure on
[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}
, for all
q\in[1,r)
and
{\nu}^{*}\in L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
,

\|{\nu}^{*}\|_{L_{q}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}\leq\hat{{\lambda}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R})^{(r-q)/(rq)}\|{\nu}^{*}\|_{L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}.

Therefore, by taking the limit as
N\rightarrow\infty
on both sides of (
0.A.55
), and using that
r\in[4,\infty)
,
l\in\{0\}\cup[1,r/4]
and
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|{\nu}^{N}-{\nu}\|_{L_{r}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R},\hat{{\lambda}};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}=0
, and then by using (
0.A.53
) and (
0.A.54
), we conclude that the limit in (
0.A.51
) holds, which completes the proof.
∎

0.A.5
Proof of Proposition
8.12

In this subsection, we prove Proposition
8.12

using a standard Picard–Lindelöf iteration argument. Recall the definition of
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
from the beginning of Section
3.2
. Since
{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
is a Banach space,
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
is also a Banach space when equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}
(see e.g.
[
43
, Proposition 1.2.29]
). Recall that
f^{*}=(f^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
is the initial condition of the system of PDEs (
2.25
). By Assumption
6
(i),
f^{*}\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
. Take
T_{2}>T_{1}\geq 0
, and let

\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\left\{{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})):\;\sup_{t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]}\|{\phi}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}<\infty\right\}.

Define the norm
\|\cdot\|_{L_{\infty}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))}:\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))\rightarrow[0,\infty)
given for all
{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
by

\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\sup_{t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]}\|{\phi}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})},

(0.A.56)

where the equality follows from the fact that
{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
. Since
L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
is a Banach space, the vector space
\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
is also a Banach space when equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{L_{\infty}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}
(see e.g.
[
62
, Theorem 43.6]
). Recall the definition of the semigroup
\{P_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
of Brownian motion run at speed
m
in (
3.4
), and recall the definition of the reaction term
F^{*}:{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}\times{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
in (
2.26
). Let
u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
be the continuous mild solution to (
2.11
) given in Proposition
7.2
. For all
T_{2}>T_{1}\geq 0
,
g\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
,
{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
,
x\in\mathbb{R}
and
t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]
, we define

(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi})(x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(P_{t-T_{1}}g)(x)+\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\Big(P_{t-{\tau}}F^{*}(u({\tau},\cdot),{\phi}({\tau},\cdot))\Big)(x)\,d{\tau}.

(0.A.57)

Lemma 0.A.12
.

Suppose that
(s_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
,
q_{+}
and
q_{-}
satisfy Assumptions
2
and
3
, that
{\mu}\in(0,1)
and
m>0
, that
f
satisfies Assumption
4
and
5
, and that the reaction term
F=(F_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
defined in (
2.12
) is monostable in the sense of Definition
2.3
. Let
u=(u_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
be the continuous mild solution to (
2.11
) given in Proposition
7.2
. For all
T_{2}>T_{1}\geq 0
and
g\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, the following properties hold:

(i)

H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi}\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
for all
t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]
and
{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
.

(ii)

(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi})_{t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
for all
{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
.

Moreover, there exists
C=C(q_{+},q_{-})>0
such that for all
T_{2}>T_{1}\geq 0
,
g\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
and
{\phi},{\psi}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]}\left\|(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi})-(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\psi})\right\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}

(0.A.58)

\displaystyle\leq C(T_{2}-T_{1})\sup_{t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]}\left\|{\phi}(t,\cdot)-{\psi}(t,\cdot)\right\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}.

Proof.

To establish assertion (i), observe that by the definition of the reaction term
F^{*}=(F^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}\times{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
in (
2.26
), the fact that
s_{k}\leq 1\,\forall k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by Assumption
2
(i) and (iii), and using that
q_{+}
and
q_{-}
are non-negative on
[0,\infty)
by Assumption
3
, we conclude that for all
z\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
and
z^{*}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\|F^{*}(z,z^{*})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|(s_{k}(1-{\mu})z^{*}_{k}+\mathds{1}_{\{k\geq 1\}}s_{k-1}{\mu}z^{*}_{k-1})q_{+}(\|z\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})-z^{*}_{k}q_{-}(\|z\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})\right|

(0.A.59)

\displaystyle\leq\|z^{*}\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}(q_{+}(\|z\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})+q_{-}(\|z\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})).

Also, since
q_{+},q_{-}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
are polynomials by Assumption
3
, Lemma
7.6
implies that

\sup_{t\geq 0}\|q_{+}(\|u(t,\cdot)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})+q_{-}(\|u(t,\cdot)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}\leq\|q_{+}+q_{-}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,1];\mathbb{R})}.

(0.A.60)

For
(t,x)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
, recall the definition of the Gaussian kernel
p(t,x)
in (
3.3
). For all
T_{2}>T_{1}\geq 0
,
g\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
,
{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
,
t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\|(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi})(x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}

(0.A.61)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-T_{1},x-y)\|g(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\,dy+\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-{\tau},x-y)\|F^{*}(u({\tau},y),{\phi}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\,dy\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\|g\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}+\|q_{+}+q_{-}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,1];\mathbb{R})}\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-{\tau},x-y)\|{\phi}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\,dy\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\|g\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}+\|q_{+}+q_{-}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,1];\mathbb{R})}(T_{2}-T_{1})\|{\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))},

where for the second inequality we used (
0.A.59
) and (
0.A.60
). Since
{\phi}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
, the right-hand side of (
0.A.61
) is finite. Observing that (
0.A.61
) holds for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we conclude that assertion (i) holds.

In order to establish assertion (ii), we notice that by combining (
0.A.59
), (
0.A.60
) and the definition of
H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi}
in (
0.A.57
),
we can apply estimate (
0.A.18
) from Lemma
0.A.7
to conclude that for all
{\delta}\in(0,T_{2}-T_{1})
, there exists
C_{{\delta},g,T_{1},T_{2},{\phi}}>0
such that for all
t,t^{\prime}\in[T_{1}+{\delta},T_{2}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\|(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi})(x)-(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t^{\prime}){\phi})(x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\leq C_{{\delta},g,T_{1},T_{2},{\phi}}|t^{\prime}-t|^{1/2}.

We then conclude that

\|H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi}-H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t^{\prime}){\phi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}\leq C_{{\delta},g,T_{1},T_{2},{\phi}}|t^{\prime}-t|^{1/2},

and therefore
(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi})_{t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]}\in\mathscr{C}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
. Hence, by observing that (
0.A.61
) holds for all
t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, the proof of assertion (ii) is complete.

It remains to establish (
0.A.58
). Observe that by the definition of the reaction term
F^{*}=(F^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}\times{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
in (
2.26
), the fact that
s_{k}\leq 1\,\forall k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by Assumption
2
(i) and (iii), and using that
q_{+}
and
q_{-}
are non-negative on
[0,\infty)
by Assumption
3
, we have that for all
z\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
and
z^{*},z^{\prime}\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\|F^{*}(z,z^{*})-F^{*}(z,z^{\prime})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}

(0.A.62)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left|(s_{k}(1-{\mu})(z^{*}_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k})+\mathds{1}_{\{k\geq 1\}}s_{k-1}{\mu}(z^{*}_{k-1}-z^{\prime}_{k-1}))q_{+}(\|z\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})-(z^{*}_{k}-z^{\prime}_{k})q_{-}(\|z\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|z^{*}-z^{\prime}\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}(q_{+}(\|z\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})+q_{-}(\|z\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})).

Then, for all
{\phi},{\psi}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
,
g\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
,
t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\|(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\phi})(x)-(H(T_{1},T_{2},g,t){\psi})(x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}

(0.A.63)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-{\tau},x-y)\|F^{*}(u({\tau},y),{\phi}({\tau},y))-F^{*}(u({\tau},y),{\psi}({\tau},y))\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\,dy\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\|q_{+}+q_{-}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,1];\mathbb{R})}\int_{T_{1}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t-{\tau},x-y)\|{\phi}({\tau},y)-{\psi}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\,dy\,d{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\|q_{+}+q_{-}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,1];\mathbb{R})}(t-T_{1})\|{\phi}-{\psi}\|_{L_{\infty}((T_{1},T_{2}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))},

where for the second inequality we used (
0.A.62
) and (
0.A.60
). By taking the supremum over
t\in(T_{1},T_{2}]
on both sides of (
0.A.63
), we conclude that (
0.A.58
) holds, which completes the proof.
∎

We are finally ready to prove Proposition
8.12
.

Proof of Proposition
8.12
.

Let
C=C(q_{+},q_{-})>0
be such that estimate (
0.A.58
) from Lemma
0.A.12
holds for any
T_{2}>T_{1}\geq 0
and
g\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, and take
T^{*}>0
sufficiently small so that
CT^{*}<1
.
Since by Assumption
6
(i),
f^{*}=(f^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, Lemma
0.A.12
implies that
{\phi}\mapsto H(0,T^{*},f^{*},\cdot){\phi}
is a contraction mapping on
\mathscr{C}_{b}((0,T^{*}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
.
Hence, by the completeness of
\mathscr{C}_{b}((0,T^{*}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
, we can apply Banach’s fixed-point theorem (see e.g.
[
19
, Theorem 5.7]
) to conclude that there exists a unique
u^{*}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((0,T^{*}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
such that
(H(0,T^{*},f^{*},t)u^{*})(\cdot)=u^{*}(t,\cdot)
for all
t\in(0,T^{*}]
.

Since
u^{*}(T,\cdot)\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, we can use the same argument to define
u^{*}
on the time interval
(0,2T^{*}]
. Repeating this argument iteratively (which is allowed since the constant
C>0
in estimate (
0.A.58
) from Lemma
0.A.12
does not depend on the particular time interval), we can define
u^{*}
globally in time, i.e. there is a unique
u^{*}:(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
u^{*}|_{(nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}]}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
and

u^{*}|_{(nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}]}=H\Big(nT^{*},\,(n+1)T^{*},\,u^{*}(nT^{*},\cdot),\,\cdot\Big)\,\Big(u^{*}|_{(nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}]}\Big).

(0.A.64)

Since this argument is standard in the construction of solutions of both finite and infinite-dimensional dynamical systems, we omit the details and refer the interested reader to e.g.
[
49
, Theorem 2.2.2]
).

For
T=0
, we then define
u^{*}(0,\cdot)\equiv f^{*}(\cdot)
. Then, by the semigroup property of the heat kernel
\{P_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
defined in (
3.4
), the function
u^{*}:(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
constructed in this way satisfies (
8.78
) for all
(T,x)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
, i.e.
u^{*}=(u^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
satisfies assertion (i). Since for all
T\in(0,\infty)
,
u^{*}|_{(0,T]}\in L_{\infty}(0,T]\times\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, by applying Proposition
7.2
(iii) and estimate (
0.A.59
), we conclude that for all
T\in(0,\infty)
, the map

(F^{*}(u(t,x),u^{*}(t,x)))_{t\in[0,T],\,x\in\mathbb{R}}\in L_{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}).

By using (
8.78
) and estimate (
0.A.18
) from Lemma
0.A.7
, it follows that
u^{*}\in\mathscr{C}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})\cap\mathscr{C}((0,\infty);L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
. Hence, recalling that by Assumption
6
(i),
f^{*}\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
and that
u^{*}(0,\cdot)=f^{*}(\cdot)
, we conclude that
u^{*}
also satisfies assertions (ii) and (iii).

We will now establish that
u^{*}=(u^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
satisfies assertion (iv). By the semigroup property of the heat kernel
\{P_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
defined in (
3.4
) and by assertion (i) of this proposition, observe that
for all
{\delta}>0
,
t>{\delta}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

u^{*}(t,x)=(P_{t-{\delta}}u^{*}({\delta},\cdot))(x)+\int_{{\delta}}^{t}\Big(P_{t-{\tau}}F^{*}(u({\tau},\cdot),u^{*}({\tau},\cdot))\Big)(x)\,d{\tau}.

(0.A.65)

Since
u^{*}({\delta},\cdot)\in\mathscr{C}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, by the standard theory for linear parabolic PDEs (see e.g.
[
52
, Theorem 8.10.1]
), assertion (iv) will be proved after establishing that for all
T>{\delta}>0
, there exists
C_{{\delta},T}>0
such that for all
t_{1},t_{2}\in[{\delta},T]
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\|F^{*}(u(t_{1},x_{1}),u^{*}(t_{1},x_{1}))-F^{*}(u(t_{2},x_{2}),u^{*}(t_{2},x_{2}))\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\leq C_{{\delta},T}(|x_{1}-x_{2}|+|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{1/2}).

(0.A.66)

Observe that by combining assertions (i) and (ii) of this proposition with (
0.A.59
) and estimate (
0.A.18
) from Lemma
0.A.7
, we have that for all
T>{\delta}>0
, there exists
C^{(1)}_{{\delta},T}>0
such that for all
t_{1},t_{2}\in[{\delta},T]
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\|u^{*}(t_{1},x_{1})-u^{*}(t_{2},x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\leq C^{(1)}_{{\delta},T}(|x_{1}-x_{2}|+|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{1/2}).

(0.A.67)

Take
T>{\delta}>0
. By the definition of the reaction term
F^{*}=(F^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in (
2.26
), the triangle inequality and the fact that
s_{k}\leq 1\;\forall k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by Assumption
2
(i) and (iii), we have that for all
t_{1},t_{2}\in[{\delta},T]
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\|F^{*}(u(t_{1},x_{1}),u^{*}(t_{1},x_{1}))-F^{*}(u(t_{2},x_{2}),u^{*}(t_{2},x_{2}))\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}

(0.A.68)

\displaystyle\leq(q_{+}(\|u(t_{1},x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})+q_{-}(\|u(t_{1},x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}))\|u^{*}(t_{1},x_{1})-u^{*}(t_{2},x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\|u^{*}(t_{2},x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\Big(|q_{+}(\|u(t_{1},x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})-q_{+}(\|u(t_{2},x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})|

\displaystyle+|q_{-}(\|u(t_{1},x_{1})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})-q_{-}(\|u(t_{2},x_{2})\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})|\Big).

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.68
), we use (
0.A.67
) and estimate (
0.A.60
), and for the second term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.68
) we use the fact that
q_{+},q_{-}:[0,\infty)\rightarrow[0,\infty)
are polynomials, combined with Lemma
7.5
, to deduce that estimate (
0.A.66
) holds, which completes the proof of assertion (iv).

We will now show that
u^{*}=(u^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
is the unique function satisfying assertions (i)–(iv). Let
\tilde{u}^{*}=(\tilde{u}^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
be such that
\tilde{u}^{*}
satisfies assertions (i)–(iv). Then, by (
8.78
), Proposition
7.2
(iii), (
0.A.59
) and estimate (
0.A.18
) from Lemma
0.A.7
, we conclude that for all
T\in(0,\infty)
,
\tilde{u}^{*}\in\mathscr{C}_{b}((0,T];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
. Moreover, by (
8.78
) and (
0.A.57
),
\tilde{u}^{*}
is such that for all
T\in(0,\infty)
,

\tilde{u}^{*}|_{(0,T]}=H(0,T,f^{*},\cdot)\Big(\tilde{u}^{*}|_{(0,T]}\Big).

Let
T^{*}\in(0,\infty)
be the parameter defined at the beginning of the proof of this proposition. As explained at the beginning of the proof of this proposition, from Lemma
0.A.12
,
{\phi}\mapsto H(0,T^{*},f^{*},\cdot){\phi}
is a contraction mapping on
\mathscr{C}_{b}((0,T^{*}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
, and therefore by Banach’s fixed-point theorem, we conclude that

\tilde{u}^{*}|_{(0,T^{*}]}=u^{*}|_{(0,T^{*}]}.

Repeating this argument iteratively we conclude that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\tilde{u}^{*}|_{(nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}]}=H\Big(nT^{*},\,(n+1)T^{*},\,u^{*}(nT^{*},\cdot),\,\cdot\Big)\,\Big(\tilde{u}^{*}|_{(nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}]}\Big).

Therefore by using the fact that
{\phi}\mapsto H(nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*},u^{*}(nT^{*},\cdot),\cdot){\phi}
is a contraction mapping on
\mathscr{C}_{b}((nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}];L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}))
by Lemma
0.A.12
and the definition of
T^{*}
at the beginning of the proof of this proposition, we can apply Banach’s fixed point theorem and (
0.A.64
) to conclude that

\tilde{u}^{*}|_{(nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}]}=u^{*}|_{(nT^{*},(n+1)T^{*}]}\quad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}_{0},

which implies that
\tilde{u}^{*}=u^{*}
, and therefore uniqueness holds.

To complete the proof of Proposition
8.12
, it remains to establish (
8.79
). For this purpose, we will use an induction argument on
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Define the function
h_{0}:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

h_{0}(t,x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(1-{\mu})q_{+}(\|u(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})-q_{-}(\|u(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})\quad\forall\,(t,x)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}.

By Assumption
6
(iii), we have that
0\leq u^{*}_{0}(0,x)\leq u_{0}(0,x)\;\forall\,x\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, by recalling the definition of the reaction terms
F=(F_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
and
F^{*}=(F^{*}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in (
2.12
) and (
2.26
), respectively, and using Proposition
7.2
(i) and (iv), and assertions (i) and (iv) of this proposition, we conclude that for all
(t,x)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
,

{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}u^{*}_{0}(t,x)-\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}u^{*}_{0}(t,x)-u^{*}_{0}(t,x)h_{0}(t,x)={\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}u_{0}(t,x)-\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}u_{0}(t,x)-u_{0}(t,x)h_{0}(t,x).

Hence, by classical comparison theorems for parabolic PDEs (see e.g.
[
5
, Proposition 2.1]
), we conclude that
0\leq u^{*}_{0}(t,x)\leq u_{0}(t,x)\;\forall\,(t,x)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
.

Suppose now that (
8.79
) holds for some
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Define the function
h_{k+1}:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

h_{k+1}(t,x)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=s_{k+1}(1-{\mu})q_{+}(\|u(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})-q_{-}(\|u(t,x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})\quad\forall\,(t,x)\in[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}.

By Assumption
6
(iii), we conclude that
0\leq u^{*}_{k+1}(0,x)\leq u_{k+1}(0,x)\;\forall\,x\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, by using (
2.12
) and (
2.26
) again, and then applying Proposition
7.2
(i) and (iv), assertions (i) and (iv) of this proposition, and by our induction hypothesis, we conclude that for all
(t,x)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}u^{*}_{k+1}(t,x)-\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}u^{*}_{k+1}(t,x)-u^{*}_{k+1}(t,x)h_{k+1}(t,x)-s_{k}{\mu}u_{k}(t,x)q_{+}(\|u\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}u_{k+1}(t,x)-\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}u_{k+1}(t,x)-u_{k+1}(t,x)h_{k+1}(t,x)-s_{k}{\mu}u_{k}(t,x)q_{+}(\|u\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}).

Hence, again using a standard comparison principle, (
8.79
) holds when
k
is replaced by
k+1
, which completes the proof.
∎

0.A.6
Proof of Lemmas
3.7
,
4.2
and
5.4

In this subsection, we will prove three lemmas used in Sections
3
,
4
and
5
whose proofs follow from standard arguments. We start by proving Lemma
3.7
.

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

We must establish that
u:[0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
satisfies Definition
3.4
(i)-(iv). Definition
3.4
(i) follows directly from condition (i). By condition (ii) and by recalling estimate (
7.5
), we conclude that for any
T>0
,

F(u)|_{[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}}\in L_{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}).

(0.A.69)

In particular,
F(u)\in L_{1,\textrm{loc}}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, and
u
satisfies Definition
3.4
(iii). Observe also that by condition (iii), and then by Assumption
4
(ii), (
0.A.69
) and Jensen’s inequality, we conclude that for all
T>0
,

\|u(T,\cdot)\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}\leq\|f\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}+T\|F(u)\|_{L_{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}<\infty.

In particular, for all
T\geq 0
,
u(T,\cdot)\in L_{1,\textrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
, and therefore
u
satisfies Definition
3.4
(ii).

It remains to establish that
u
satisfies Definition
3.4
(iv). Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{1,2}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
and
T>0
be arbitrary. As explained after Definition
3.4
, it will suffice to show that (
3.2
) holds. Note that by condition (iii),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}u_{k}(t,x)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt

(0.A.70)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{t}f_{k})(x)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{0}^{t}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt.

We will tackle the terms on the right-hand side of (
0.A.70
) separately. For the first term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.70
), fix
{\varepsilon}\in(0,T)
and note that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{t}f_{k})(x)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt

(0.A.71)

\displaystyle=\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{t}f_{k})(x)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{t}f_{k})(x)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt.

Since
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{1,2}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
, there exists
R_{{\varphi}}\in(0,\infty)
such that
\supp{\varphi}\subset[0,\infty)\times[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{{\varphi}}]
. Moreover, there exists
C_{{\varphi}}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\sup_{t\in[0,\infty)}\left(\|{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}{\varphi}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}+\frac{m}{2}\|\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}{\varphi}(t,\cdot)\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}\right)\leq C_{{\varphi}}.

(0.A.72)

Therefore, by applying Jensen’s inequality, we can bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.71
) by

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{t}f_{k})(x)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt\right|\leq 2{\varepsilon}R_{{\varphi}}C_{{\varphi}}\|f_{k}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}\leq 2{\varepsilon}R_{{\varphi}}C_{{\varphi}}\|f\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}.

(0.A.73)

For
(t,x)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
, recall the definition of the Gaussian kernel
p(t,x)
in (
3.3
). For the first term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.71
), by applying (
3.4
) and Fubini’s theorem, we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{t}f_{k})(x)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt

(0.A.74)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{k}(y)\left(\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t,x-y)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt\right)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{k}(y)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big(p(T,x-y){\varphi}(T,x)-p({\varepsilon},x-y){\varphi}({\varepsilon},x)\Big)\,dx\right)dy

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{k}(y)\left(\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(-{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right)p(t,x-y){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt\right)dy

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{T}f_{k})(x){\varphi}(T,x)\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{k}(y)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}p({\varepsilon},x-y){\varphi}({\varepsilon},x)\,dx\right)dy,

where for the second identity we used integration by parts, while for the third equality we used (
3.4
), Fubini’s theorem, and the fact that by (
3.3
),
\left(-{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right)p(t,\cdot)\equiv 0
for
t>0
. We now claim that

\lim_{{\varepsilon}\downarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{k}(y)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}p({\varepsilon},x-y){\varphi}({\varepsilon},x)\,dx\right)dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{k}(y){\varphi}(0,y)\,dy.

(0.A.75)

To establish (
0.A.75
), we first notice that since
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
, by using standard regularity properties of the heat kernel (see e.g.
[
30
, Theorem 2.3.1(iii)]
), we have

\lim_{{\varepsilon}\downarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p({\varepsilon},x-y){\varphi}({\varepsilon},x)\,dx={\varphi}(0,y)\quad\forall\,y\in\mathbb{R},

and therefore, since
f\in L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})
by Assumption
4
(ii), by the dominated convergence theorem, the limit in (
0.A.75
) will be proved after establishing that for all
{\varepsilon}\in(0,T)
and
y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}p({\varepsilon},x-y){\varphi}({\varepsilon},x)\,dx\right|

(0.A.76)

\displaystyle\leq\mathds{1}_{\{y\in[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{{\varphi}}]\}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}+\mathds{1}_{\{y\not\in[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{{\varphi}}]\}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}e^{-{(|y|-R_{\varphi})^{2}}/{2mT}},

recalling that we chose
R_{{\varphi}}\in(0,\infty)
such that
\supp{\varphi}\subset[0,\infty)\times[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{\varphi}]
. Now, observe that (
0.A.76
) follows from Jensen’s inequality and the elementary identity
\int_{\mathbb{R}}p(t,z)\,dz=1\;\forall t>0
for
y\in[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{\varphi}]
, and for
y\not\in[-R_{{\varphi}},R_{\varphi}]
,

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}}p({\varepsilon},x-y){\varphi}({\varepsilon},x)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}\int_{\mathbb{R}}p({\varepsilon},x-y)\mathds{1}_{\{x\in[-R_{\varphi},R_{\varphi}]\}}\,dx

\displaystyle=\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}\int_{|y|-R_{{\varphi}}}^{|y|+R_{{\varphi}}}p({\varepsilon},z)\,dz

\displaystyle\leq\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}e^{-{(|y|-R_{\varphi})^{2}}/{2mT}}.

Hence, (
0.A.76
) holds, and therefore (
0.A.75
) also holds. By applying (
0.A.73
) and (
0.A.74
) to (
0.A.71
), and then by observing that we can take
{\varepsilon}\in(0,T)
to be arbitrarily small and using (
0.A.75
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{t}f_{k})(x)\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\,dx\,dt=\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{T}f_{k})(x){\varphi}(T,x)\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}}f_{k}(x){\varphi}(0,x)dx.

(0.A.77)

We now tackle the second term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.70
). Fix
{\varepsilon}\in(0,T)
, and observe that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{0}^{t}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt

(0.A.78)

\displaystyle=\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{0}^{t-{\varepsilon}}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{t-{\varepsilon}}^{t}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{0}^{t}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt.

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.78
), by using integration by parts,

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{0}^{t-{\varepsilon}}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt

(0.A.79)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(T,x)\left(\int_{0}^{T-{\varepsilon}}(P_{T-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx

\displaystyle+\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(t,x)\left(-{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{t-{\varepsilon}}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(T,x)\left(\int_{0}^{T-{\varepsilon}}(P_{T-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx-\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(t,x)(P_{{\varepsilon}}F_{k}(u(t-{\varepsilon},\cdot)))(x)\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle+\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{0}^{t-{\varepsilon}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(-{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right)p(t-{\tau},x-y)F_{k}(u({\tau},y))\,dy\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(T,x)\left(\int_{0}^{T-{\varepsilon}}(P_{T-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx-\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(t,x)(P_{{\varepsilon}}F_{k}(u(t-{\varepsilon},\cdot)))(x)\,dx\,dt,

where for the second equality we used (
3.4
), (
3.3
), the fact that
p(t,x)
has uniformly bounded derivatives of all orders on
[{\varepsilon},\infty)\times\mathbb{R}
, (
0.A.69
) and the dominated convergence theorem, and for the third equality we used the fact that
\left(-{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right)p(t,\cdot)\equiv 0
for
t>0
. We will now compute the limit for both terms on the right-hand side of (
0.A.79
) as
{\varepsilon}\downarrow 0
. By (
0.A.69
), we have that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, the map

[0,T]\ni t\mapsto\int_{0}^{t}(P_{T-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}

is continuous, and therefore by (
0.A.69
) and the dominated convergence theorem,

\lim_{{\varepsilon}\downarrow 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(T,x)\left(\int_{0}^{T-{\varepsilon}}(P_{T-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(T,x)\left(\int_{0}^{T}(P_{T-t}F_{k}(u(t,\cdot)))(x)\,dt\right)dx.

(0.A.80)

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.79
), using Fubini’s theorem, and then by using (
0.A.69
) and the same argument we used to derive (
0.A.75
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\lim_{{\varepsilon}\downarrow 0}\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(t,x)(P_{{\varepsilon}}F_{k}(u(t,\cdot)))(x)\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle=\lim_{{\varepsilon}\downarrow 0}\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(P_{{\varepsilon}}{\varphi}(t,\cdot))(x)F_{k}(u(t,x))\,dx\,dt

(0.A.81)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(t,x)F_{k}(u(t,x))\,dx\,dt.

Recall from after (
0.A.71
) that
R_{\varphi}\in(0,\infty)
is such that
\supp{\varphi}\subset[0,\infty)\times[-R_{\varphi},R_{\varphi}]
. For the second and third terms on the right-hand side of (
0.A.78
), observe that by (
0.A.69
) and since
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{1,2}_{c}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|

\displaystyle\int_{\varepsilon}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{t-{\varepsilon}}^{t}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt

(0.A.82)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{0}^{t}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 4R_{\varphi}T{\varepsilon}\left\|\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}\right\|_{L_{\infty}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})}\|F(u)\|_{L_{\infty}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R};{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1})}.

By applying (
0.A.79
) and (
0.A.78
), and then by using the fact that we can take
{\varepsilon}\in(0,T)
to be arbitrarily small, and using (
0.A.80
), (
0.A.81
) and (
0.A.82
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left({\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}_{t}+\frac{m}{2}\mathop{}\!\mathbin{\bigtriangleup}\right){\varphi}(t,x)\left(\int_{0}^{t}(P_{t-{\tau}}F_{k}(u({\tau},\cdot)))(x)\,d{\tau}\right)dx\,dt

(0.A.83)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(T,x)\left(\int_{0}^{T}(P_{T-t}F_{k}(u(t,\cdot)))(x)\,dt\right)dx-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\varphi}(t,x)F_{k}(u(t,x))\,dx\,dt.

Finally, applying (
0.A.77
) and (
0.A.83
) to (
0.A.70
), and using condition (iii), we conclude that (
3.2
) holds for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}_{c}^{1,2}([0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
and
T>0
, which completes the proof.
∎

Next, we establish Lemma
4.2
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

We will divide the proof into steps corresponding to each of the assertions (i) - (vi).

Proof of assertion (i):

We start by establishing that
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
. Note that by (
4.5
), there exists
C_{x,T}^{N}>0
such that for all
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
and
t\in[0,T]
,

{\phi}^{N,T,x}(t,y)=p^{N}(T-t,x-y)\leq C_{x,T}^{N}\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{2}}.

(0.A.84)

Therefore, by (
4.9
) and (
4.4
), and then by using (
0.A.84
) and Proposition
3.1
(i) and (ii), we can use dominated convergence and the fact that
p^{N}(\cdot,x)\in\mathscr{C}([0,T];\mathbb{R})
for all
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
, to conclude that for all
(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{N}
and
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
, the following limit holds:

\displaystyle\lim_{(t^{*},\boldsymbol{{\xi}}^{*})\rightarrow(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t^{*},\boldsymbol{{\xi}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\lim_{(t^{*},\boldsymbol{{\xi}}^{*})\rightarrow(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}^{*}_{k}(y)}{N}p^{N}(T-t^{*},x-y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\lim_{(t^{*},\boldsymbol{{\xi}}^{*})\rightarrow(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}\frac{{\xi}^{*}_{k}(y)}{N}p^{N}(T-t^{*},x-y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}_{k}(y)}{N}p^{N}(T-t,x-y)

\displaystyle=g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}).

Hence,
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
. In order to characterise
\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}
, we observe that (
4.4
) implies that for all
t\in(0,T)
and
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,

\left(\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}{\phi}^{N,T,x}(\cdot,y)\right)(t)=\frac{m_{N}}{2}\Big(2p^{N}(T-t,y-x)-p^{N}(T-t,y-L_{N}^{-1}-x)-p^{N}(T-t,y+L_{N}^{-1}-x)\Big).

(0.A.85)

By using (
4.9
), (
0.A.85
), (
0.A.84
), and the definition of
\mathcal{S}^{N}
in (
2.3
), we conclude by dominated convergence that for all
t\in(0,T)
and
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(y))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\right)(t)

\displaystyle=\Bigg\langle\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\,\frac{{\xi}_{k}(\cdot)}{N},\,\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}{\phi}^{N,T,x}(t,\cdot)\Bigg\rangle_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{N}}{2}\Big(2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}-g^{N,T,x-L_{N}^{-1}}_{\mathcal{I}}-g^{N,T,x+L_{N}^{-1}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}),

where the last equality follows from (
0.A.85
) and the fact that by (
0.A.84
) and (
2.3
), the series defining the map
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}
is absolutely convergent. This gives us identity (
4.12
). The desired continuity of the map
(0,T)\times\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\mapsto\left(\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(\cdot,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\right)(t)
then follows from the continuity of
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}
. This concludes the proof of assertion (i).

Proof of assertion (ii):

For any
t\in[0,T]
and
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}(y))_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}},\boldsymbol{{\zeta}}=({\zeta}(y))_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
, we have, from the definition of
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
4.9
),

\displaystyle\Big|g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{NL_{N}}\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\|{\xi}(y)-{\zeta}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{x,N}\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\frac{\|{\xi}(y)-{\zeta}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{(1+|y|)^{2(1+\deg q_{-})}},

where the last estimate follows from (
0.A.84
). Therefore, by the definition of
\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
in (
2.5
) for any
t\in[0,T]
, the map
\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\mapsto g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is in
\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
, as desired.

We now establish (
4.13
). Recall from before (
2.5
) that for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
, we let
\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}
denote the configuration in
\mathcal{S}^{N}
consisting of exactly one particle carrying
k
mutations at deme
y
. By the definition of
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
4.9
), we have the following identities, for any
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,
k\in\mathcal{I}
,
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{j}(z))_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},z\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
and
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y+L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big)

(0.A.86)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NL_{N}}\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big(p^{N}(T-t,y+L_{N}^{-1}-x)-p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\Big),

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y-L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NL_{N}}\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big(p^{N}(T-t,y-L_{N}^{-1}-x)-p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\Big),

\displaystyle g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)=\frac{1}{NL_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x),

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\right)-g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big)=-\frac{1}{NL_{N}}\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x).

Combining the definition of the infinitesimal generator
\mathcal{L}^{N}
given in (
2.6
) and in (
2
), with (
0.A.86
), we conclude that for all
t\in[0,T]
and all
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(y))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\cdot)\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})

(0.A.87)

\displaystyle=\frac{m_{N}}{2}\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}}\,\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\,\frac{{\xi}_{k}(y)}{NL_{N}}\Big(p^{N}(T-t,y+L_{N}^{-1}-x)+p^{N}(T-t,y-L_{N}^{-1}-x)

\displaystyle-2p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}}\,\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\,\frac{1}{L_{N}}F_{k}\left(\frac{{\xi}(y)}{N}\right)p^{N}(T-t,y-x),

where
F=(F_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
is the reaction term defined in (
2.12
). Since (
2.3
) implies that, for all
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}(y))_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,
\|{\xi}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}
grows at most polynomially in
|y|
as
|y-x|\rightarrow\infty
, it follows from (
4.5
) that the series on the right-hand side of (
0.A.87
) are absolutely convergent uniformly in
t\in[0,T]
. This ensures the desired continuity and justifies rearranging the terms of the series to obtain (
4.13
). This concludes the proof of assertion (ii).

Proof of assertion (iii):

We must first verify that the map
[0,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\mapsto\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big((g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\cdot)\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is well defined and continuous. By the first identity in (
0.A.86
), and using the definition of
\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}
in (
4.11
), note that, for any
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,
k\in\mathcal{I}
,
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{j}(z))_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},z\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
and
t\in[0,T]
with
{\xi}_{k}(y)>0
,

\displaystyle(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y+L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)

(0.A.88)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NL_{N}^{2}}\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y+L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)+g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big)\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NL_{N}^{2}}\left(2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)+\frac{1}{NL_{N}^{2}}\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\right)\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)

\displaystyle=\frac{2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}{NL_{N}^{2}}\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{4}}\left(\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\right)^{2}.

By a similar argument, the following identities hold for any
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,
k\in\mathcal{I}
,
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{j}(z))_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},z\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
and
t\in[0,T]
:

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big((g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y-L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big)

(0.A.89)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Bigg(-\frac{2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}{NL_{N}^{2}}\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-L_{N}^{-1}-x)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{4}}\left(\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-L_{N}^{-1}-x\right)^{2}\Bigg),

\displaystyle(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)}{NL_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)^{2},

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big((g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\left(-\frac{2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)}{NL_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)^{2}\right).

Similarly to the proof of assertion (ii), since (
2.3
) implies that, for all
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}(y))_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,
\|{\xi}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}
grows at most polynomially in
|y|
as
|y-x|\rightarrow\infty
, it follows from (
2
), (
4.5
), the fact that by assertion (i),
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathscr{C}([0,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
, and from identities (
0.A.88
) and (
0.A.89
), that the series defining the action of
\mathcal{L}^{N}
on
(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}
is absolutely convergent uniformly over
t\in[0,T]
. This ensures that the map
[0,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\mapsto\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big((g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\cdot)\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is well defined and continuous. Moreover, rearranging the terms of the series, combining (
4.13
) with (
0.A.88
) and (
0.A.89
), and recalling (
4.10
), we obtain (
4.14
), as desired.

Proof of assertion (iv):

We will bound the supremum, over
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]
, of the expectation of each term on the left-hand side of (
4.15
) separately. Starting with the first term, by the definition of
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
4.9
) and by Lemma
4.1
, we conclude that for any
r\geq 1
,

\sup_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\;\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[(g^{N,T,y}_{\mathcal{I}})^{r}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\Big]<\infty.

(0.A.90)

In particular, by (
4.12
), estimate (
0.A.90
) implies that for any
r\geq 1
,

\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(\cdot,{\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\Bigg)^{r}(t_{1})\Bigg]<\infty.

(0.A.91)

To bound the third term on the left-hand side of (
4.15
), we apply the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and then estimates (
0.A.90
) and (
0.A.91
), obtaining

\displaystyle\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\Bigg(\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(\cdot,{\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\Bigg)^{2}(t_{1})\Bigg]

(0.A.92)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{4}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\Big]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}}{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 64\relax}}}t}g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(\cdot,{\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\Bigg)^{4}(t_{1})\Bigg]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

For the fourth term on the left-hand side of (
4.15
), by the definition of
F=(F_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in (
2.12
) and
F^{+}=(F_{k}^{+})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in (
4.10
), and since
q_{+}
and
q_{-}
are non-negative polynomials by Assumption
3
, and then in the last line since
s_{k}\leq 1\;\forall\,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by Assumption
2
(i) and (iii), we have that for any
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(y))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
and any
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F_{k}\left(\frac{{\xi}(y)}{N}\right)\Bigg|

(0.A.93)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}\left(\frac{{\xi}(y)}{N}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\Bigg(q_{+}\left(\frac{\|{\xi}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}\right)\left(s_{k}(1-{\mu})\frac{{\xi}_{k}(y)}{N}+\mathds{1}_{\{k\geq 1\}}s_{k-1}{\mu}\frac{{\xi}_{k-1}(y)}{N}\right)+q_{-}\left(\frac{\|{\xi}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}\right)\frac{{\xi}_{k}(y)}{N}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|{\xi}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}\left(q_{+}\left(\frac{\|{\xi}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}\right)+q_{-}\left(\frac{\|{\xi}(y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}\right)\right).

Using again the fact that
q_{+}
and
q_{-}
are polynomials, by Lemma
4.1
we conclude that for any
r\geq 1
,

\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg\langle\Bigg|\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F_{k}\left(u^{N}(t_{2},\cdot)\right)\Bigg|,{\phi}^{N,T,x}(t_{1},\cdot)\Bigg\rangle^{r}_{N}\Bigg]<\infty.

(0.A.94)

Combining identity (
4.13
) with estimates (
0.A.90
) and (
0.A.94
), we conclude that for any
r\geq 1
,

\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big|\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\Big|^{r}\Big]<\infty.

(0.A.95)

We now proceed to bound the fifth term on the left-hand side of (
4.15
). As in the derivation of (
0.A.92
), by combining the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with estimates (
0.A.90
) and (
0.A.95
), we conclude that

\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}(t_{2})\Big)^{2}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\Big)^{2}\right]<\infty.

(0.A.96)

Moreover, by (
4.3
), we have that for any
t\in[0,T]
and any
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,

p^{N}(T-t,y-x)^{2}\leq L_{N}p^{N}(T-t,y-x).

(0.A.97)

Therefore, by (
0.A.97
), (
0.A.93
) and the fact that
0\leq\deg q_{+}<\deg q_{-}
by Assumption
3
, we conclude that for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(y))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NL_{N}^{2}}\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}\left(\frac{{\xi}(y)}{N}\right)p^{N}(T-t,y-x)^{2}

(0.A.98)

\displaystyle+\frac{m_{N}}{2NL^{4}_{N}}\sum_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}_{k}(y)}{N}\left(\left(\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-L_{N}^{-1}-x)\right)^{2}+\left(\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,q_{+},q_{-}}\left\langle\left(\frac{\|{\xi}(\cdot)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}\right)^{1+\deg q_{-}},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x}(t,\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}+\sum_{x^{\prime}\in\{x,x-L_{N}^{-1},x+L_{N}^{-1}\}}\left\langle\frac{\|{\xi}(\cdot)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x^{\prime}}(t,\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}.

Combining (
4.14
), (
0.A.96
), Lemma
4.1
and estimate (
0.A.98
), we conclude that

\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t_{2}))\right]<\infty.

(0.A.99)

Hence, by (
0.A.90
), (
0.A.91
), (
0.A.92
), (
0.A.95
) and (
0.A.99
), estimate (
4.15
) holds, as desired.

Proof of assertion (v):

It follows from assertion (iv) that
(M(t))_{t\geq 0}
is an integrable process. It remains to verify that
M
satisfies the martingale property with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define
h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}:[0,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\wedge n^{2}\quad\forall(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{S}^{N}.

(0.A.100)

Recall that
t_{1}\in[0,T]
is fixed. By assertion (ii) of this lemma,
g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\in\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
. By (
0.A.100
), for all
\boldsymbol{{\xi}},\boldsymbol{{\zeta}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
and every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2n\Big|g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\Big|.

Hence, in particular,
h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\in\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore, by Theorem
3.2
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the process
(M^{(n)}(t))_{t\geq 0}
given by, for all
t\geq 0
,

M^{(n)}(t)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}(t))-h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}(0))-\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big(h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)({\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\,d{\tau}

(0.A.101)

is a càdlàg martingale with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
.

We now claim that for all
t\geq 0
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}M^{(n)}(t)=M(t)\quad\textrm{almost surely}.

(0.A.102)

Indeed, by the definition of
h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
0.A.100
), it is immediate that for any
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\boldsymbol{{\xi}})=\Big(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)^{2}(t_{1},\boldsymbol{{\xi}}).

(0.A.103)

Hence, (
0.A.102
) will be proved after verifying that for all
t\geq 0
, the following limit holds almost surely:

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\,\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big(h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)({\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\,d{\tau}=\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big((g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)({\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\,d{\tau}.

(0.A.104)

Since, by Theorem
3.2
,
({\eta}^{N}({\tau}))_{{\tau}\geq 0}
is an
\mathcal{S}^{N}
-valued càdlàg process, and since
\mathcal{S}^{N}
is a complete and separable metric space by Proposition
3.1
, by standard results in stochastic analysis (see e.g.
[
29
, Remark 3.6.4]
), for any
t\geq 0
and almost every realisation
({\eta}^{N}_{{\omega}}({\tau}))_{{\tau}\geq 0}
of
({\eta}^{N}({\tau}))_{{\tau}\geq 0}
, there exists a compact set
\mathcal{K}_{t,{\omega}}\subset\mathcal{S}^{N}
such that
{\eta}^{N}_{{\omega}}({\tau})\in\mathcal{K}_{t,{\omega}}\;\forall\,{\tau}\in[0,t]
.
By the characterisation of compact subsets of
\mathcal{S}^{N}
given by Proposition
3.1
, we conclude that for almost every realisation
({\eta}^{N}_{{\omega}}({\tau}))_{{\tau}\geq 0}
of
({\eta}^{N}({\tau}))_{{\tau}\geq 0}
,

\sup_{{\tau}\leq t}\;|||{\eta}^{N}_{{\omega}}({\tau})|||_{\mathcal{S}^{N}}<\infty
.
In particular, by (
2.3
), for almost every realisation
({\eta}^{N}_{{\omega}}({\tau}))_{{\tau}\geq 0}
of
({\eta}^{N}({\tau}))_{{\tau}\geq 0}
, there exists
A_{{\omega},t}>0
such that

\|{\eta}^{N}_{\omega}({\tau},y)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}\leq A_{{\omega},t}(1+|y|^{2})\quad\forall({\tau},y)\in[0,t]\times L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}.

(0.A.105)

We also observe that the following elementary inequality holds for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle|(a^{2}\wedge n^{2})-(b^{2}\wedge n^{2})|\leq|a^{2}-b^{2}|.

(0.A.106)

Hence, combining the definition of
h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
0.A.100
) with the identities given in (
0.A.88
) and (
0.A.89
), we conclude that for any
y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
,
k\in\mathcal{I}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\in[0,T]
, and
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{j}(z))_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},z\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big|h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y+L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big|

(0.A.107)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}{NL_{N}}\Big(p^{N}(T-t,y+L_{N}^{-1}-x)+p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{4}}\left(\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big|h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y-L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}{NL_{N}}\Big(p^{N}(T-t,y-L_{N}^{-1}-x)+p^{N}(T-t,y-x)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{4}}\left(\nabla_{L_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-L_{N}^{-1}-x)\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\Big|h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)}{NL_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)^{2},

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(y)>0\}}\Big|h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(y)}_{k}\Big)-h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)}{NL_{N}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}p^{N}(T-t,y-x)^{2}.

Using the definition of the action of the generator
\mathcal{L}^{N}
given in (
2.6
) and (
2
), together with estimates (
4.5
), (
0.A.105
) and (
0.A.107
), the limit in (
0.A.103
) and the dominated convergence theorem, we conclude that for any
t\geq 0
, the limit in (
0.A.104
) holds almost surely. This completes the proof of the claim (
0.A.102
).

Moreover, the definition of
h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
0.A.100
) and of
M^{(n)}
in (
0.A.101
) imply that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and all
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[(M^{(n)}(t))^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{4}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}(t))\Big]+(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}})^{4}(t_{1},\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N})

(0.A.108)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)({\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\,d{\tau}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg].

Observe that by Jensen’s inequality and Fubini’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)({\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\,d{\tau}\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]\leq t\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\Big]\,d{\tau}.

(0.A.109)

As in the proof of (
0.A.98
), to bound the right-hand side of (
0.A.109
), we first observe that by the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{N}
in (
2.6
) and (
2
), estimates (
0.A.97
) and (
0.A.107
), the definition of
F^{+}=(F^{+}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in (
4.10
), and the fact that
0\leq\deg q_{+}<\deg q_{-}
by Assumption
3
, for all
t_{1}\in[0,T]
,
{\tau}\in[0,T]
, and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have almost surely

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big(h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)({\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\right|

(0.A.110)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,q_{+},q_{-}}\left(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))+1\right)\sum_{x^{\prime}\in\{x,x-L_{N}^{-1},x+L_{N}^{-1}\}}\left\langle\frac{\|{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-,\cdot)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x^{\prime}}(t_{1},\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}

\displaystyle+\left(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))+1\right)\left\langle\left(\frac{\|{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-,\cdot)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}\right)^{1+\deg q_{-}},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x}(t,\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\left(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))+1\right)\sum_{x^{\prime}\in\{x,x-L_{N}^{-1},x+L_{N}^{-1}\}}\left\langle\frac{\|{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-,\cdot)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{1+\deg q_{-}}}{N},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x^{\prime}}(t_{1},\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}.

By squaring both sides of (
0.A.110
) and applying the elementary inequality

\left(\sum_{i=1}^{J}a_{J}\right)^{2}\leq J\sum_{i=1}^{J}a_{J}^{2}\quad\forall\;J\in\mathbb{N},\,(a_{i})_{i=1}^{J}\subset\mathbb{R},

and then by taking expectations, we conclude that for all
t_{1}\in[0,T]
,
{\tau}\in[0,T]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
