\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}h^{N,T,x,n}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},\cdot)\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\right]

(0.A.111)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,q_{+},q_{-}}\sum_{z\in\{0,-L_{N}^{-1},L_{N}^{-1}\}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\left\langle\frac{\|{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-,\cdot)\|^{1+\deg q_{-}}_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x+z}(t_{1},\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\left(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\right)^{2}\left\langle\frac{\|{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-,\cdot)\|^{1+\deg q_{-}}_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x+z}(t_{1},\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}^{2}\Bigg]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{z\in\{0,-L_{N}^{-1},L_{N}^{-1}\}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\left\langle\frac{\|{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-,\cdot)\|^{1+\deg q_{-}}_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x+z}(t_{1},\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\left[\left(g^{N,T,x}_{\mathcal{I}}(t_{1},{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-))\right)^{4}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\left\langle\frac{\|{\eta}^{N}({\tau}-,\cdot)\|^{1+\deg q_{-}}_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N},\,{\phi}^{N,T,x+z}(t_{1},\cdot)\right\rangle_{N}^{4}\Bigg]^{1/2}\Bigg),

where for the second inequality we used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Applying (
0.A.109
) and (
0.A.111
) to (
0.A.108
), together with estimate (
0.A.90
) and Lemma
4.1
, we conclude that for all
t\geq 0
,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[(M^{(n)}(t))^{2}\Big]<\infty.

(0.A.112)

Hence, for all
t\geq 0
, the collection of random variables
\{M^{(n)}({\tau}):\,0\leq{\tau}\leq t\textrm{ and }n\in\mathbb{N}\}
is uniformly integrable. By (
0.A.102
), we then conclude that
M
satisfies the martingale property with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
, and therefore assertion (v) holds.

Proof of assertion (vi):

We start by observing that for all
\boldsymbol{{\xi}},\boldsymbol{{\zeta}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\displaystyle(g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}}-g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-(g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}}-g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})

(0.A.113)

\displaystyle=\left((g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-(g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\right)+\left((g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-(g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\right)

\displaystyle-2\left(g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\right)g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})

\displaystyle-2g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\left(g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}}(t,\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\right).

Identity (
4.16
) then follows from combining the definition of
\mathcal{L}^{N}
in (
2.6
) and (
2
) with the identities in (
0.A.113
), (
0.A.86
), (
0.A.88
) and (
0.A.89
), and with the observation that for all
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
, the series defining
\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big((g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}}-g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\cdot)\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is absolutely convergent by (
4.5
) and (
2.3
). Since this argument is similar to the one used in the proof of (
4.13
) and (
4.14
), we omit the details. The fact that the map
[0,T_{1}\wedge T_{2}]\times\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni(t,\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\,\mapsto\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big((g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}}-g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\cdot)\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is in
\mathscr{C}([0,T_{1}\wedge T_{2}]\times\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
also follows from the fact that the series defining
\mathcal{L}^{N}\Big((g^{N,T_{1},x_{1}}_{\mathcal{I}}-g^{N,T_{2},x_{2}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(t,\cdot)\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is absolutely convergent uniformly in
t\in[0,T_{1}\wedge T_{2}]
and from the continuity of
p^{N}
. Finally, estimate (
4.19
) is proved by using (
4.16
), (
4.15
) and Lemma
4.1
. Since the proof of (
4.19
) is similar to the proof of (
0.A.112
), we omit the details.
∎

Finally, we prove Lemma
5.4
.

Proof of Lemma
5.4
.

We will divide the proof into steps corresponding to each of the assertions (i) - (v). We highlight that, since the proofs of these assertions are very similar to the proof of Lemma
4.2
, we will omit some details.

Proof of assertion (i):

Since
\supp({\varphi})\subset\mathbb{R}
is compact, and using (
5.1
), there exists
R>0
such that for
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(y))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})=\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}_{k}(x)}{N}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh.

(0.A.114)

Hence, by the triangle inequality and the fact that
{\varphi}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}_{c}(\mathbb{R})
, and then by (
2.4
), we have, for
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}(y))_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}},\boldsymbol{{\zeta}}=({\zeta}(y))_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\left|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}}{L_{N}}\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\frac{\|{\xi}(x)-{\zeta}(x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}\lesssim_{{\varphi},N}d_{\mathcal{S}^{N}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}},\boldsymbol{{\zeta}}).

Therefore, by (
2.5
) we have
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
.

Proof of assertion (ii):

Recall from before (
2.5
) that for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
, we let
\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\in(\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{N}_{0}})^{L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}
denote the configuration with only one particle carrying exactly
k
mutations in deme
x
. Then, by the definition of
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
5.1
), the following identities hold for
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{j}(y))_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,
k\in\mathcal{I}
and
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
:

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x+L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big)

(0.A.115)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NL_{N}}\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|)\Big({\varphi}(x+L_{N}^{-1}+hL_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\Big)\,dh,

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x-L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{NL_{N}}\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|)\Big({\varphi}(x-L_{N}^{-1}+hL_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\Big)\,dh,

\displaystyle g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})=\frac{1}{NL_{N}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh,

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big)=-\frac{1}{NL_{N}}\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh.

Combining (
0.A.115
), the fact that
\supp({\varphi})\subset\mathbb{R}
is compact, and that
\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty
and then the triangle inequality, we conclude that the series in (
2.6
) defining the action of
\mathcal{L}^{N}
on
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
is absolutely convergent, and that the map
\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\mapsto\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is continuous. Moreover, by using (
0.A.115
), rearranging terms and then using (
5.2
), (
5.4
) and (
2.12
), we get (
5.5
), which completes the proof of assertion (ii).

Proof of assertion (iii):

By (
5.1
) and the same argument as we used to derive (
0.A.88
) and (
0.A.89
), the following identities hold for
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{j}(y))_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,
k\in\mathcal{I}
and
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
:

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big((g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x+L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big)

(0.A.116)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Bigg(\frac{2g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}{NL_{N}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|)\Big({\varphi}(x+L_{N}^{-1}+hL_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\Big)\,dh

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}\Bigg(\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|)\Big({\varphi}(x+L_{N}^{-1}+hL_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\Big)\,dh\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg),

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big((g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x-L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Bigg(\frac{2g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}{NL_{N}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|)\Big({\varphi}(x-L_{N}^{-1}+hL_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\Big)\,dh

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}\Bigg(\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|)\Big({\varphi}(x-L_{N}^{-1}+hL_{N}^{-1})-{\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\Big)\,dh\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg),

\displaystyle(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})=\frac{2g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}{NL_{N}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}\Bigg(\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh\Bigg)^{2},

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big((g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Bigg(-\frac{2g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})}{NL_{N}}\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}\Bigg(\int_{-1}^{1}(1-|h|){\varphi}(x+hL_{N}^{-1})\,dh\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg),

where
\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\in(\mathbb{N}_{0}^{\mathbb{N}_{0}})^{L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}
denotes the configuration with only one particle carrying exactly
k
mutations in deme
x
. Then, since
\supp({\varphi})\subset\mathbb{R}
is compact and
\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty
, by Proposition
3.1
(i) and (ii), and using assertion (ii) of this lemma, the identities in (
0.A.116
) imply that the series in (
2
) defining the action of
\mathcal{L}^{N}
on
(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}
is absolutely convergent uniformly on compact subsets of
\mathcal{S}^{N}
. Therefore, the map
\mathcal{S}^{N}\ni\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\mapsto\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})
is continuous. Moreover, by using (
0.A.116
), rearranging terms and then using (
5.4
), (
5.2
), (
5.5
) and (
4.10
), we get (
5.6
), which completes the proof of assertion (iii).

Proof of assertion (iv):

We will bound each term within the expectation on the left-hand side of (
5.7
) individually. For the first term, observe that (
0.A.114
), the triangle inequality, and the fact that
\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}<\infty
imply the existence of
R>0
such that for
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}(y))_{y\in L_{N}^{-1}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})|\leq\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\frac{\|{\xi}(x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}}{N}.

(0.A.117)

We recall the following elementary inequality holds for
r\geq 1
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
(a_{i})_{i=1}^{n}\subset[0,\infty)
:

\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\Bigg)^{r}\leq n^{r-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{r}.

(0.A.118)

For
r\geq 1
, taking both sides of (
0.A.117
) to the power of
r
and then using (
0.A.118
) and that
|\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:\,|x|<R\}|\leq 2RL_{N}
, we get, for
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}(x))_{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})|^{r}\leq(2R)^{r-1}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{r}\frac{1}{L_{N}}\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\frac{\|{\xi}(x)\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}^{r}}{N^{r}}.

(0.A.119)

Taking expectations on both sides of (
0.A.119
), and then applying Theorem
3.3
, we conclude that for any
T\geq 0
and
r\geq 1
,

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(t))|^{r}\Big]<\infty.

(0.A.120)

For the second term within the expectation on the left-hand side of (
5.7
), observe that by (
5.5
), the triangle inequality, the fact that
{\varphi}
has compact support, (
5.16
) and the fact that
|F_{k}(u)|\leq F^{+}_{k}(u)
for
u\in{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}^{+}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
by (
2.12
) and (
4.10
), we conclude that there exists
R>0
such that for
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{m_{N}}{L_{N}^{3}}\|{\varphi}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}_{k}(x)}{N}

(0.A.121)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{L_{N}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}\left(\frac{{\xi}(x)}{N}\right).

For
r\geq 1
, by taking both sides of (
0.A.121
) to the power of
r
, and using (
0.A.118
) and (
0.A.93
), and then by Theorem
3.3
and the fact that by Assumption
1
we have
\displaystyle\frac{m_{N}}{L_{N}^{2}}\rightarrow m\in(0,\infty)
as
N\rightarrow\infty
, the following bound holds for
T\geq 0
:

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big|\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big|^{r}\Big]<\infty.

(0.A.122)

It remains to bound the third term within the expectation on the left-hand side of (
5.7
). By (
5.6
), the triangle inequality, (
5.18
) and the fact that
{\varphi}
has compact support, there exists
R>0
such that for
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{k}(x))_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0},x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})|\Big|\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\Big|+E(N,{\varphi},\mathcal{I},\boldsymbol{{\xi}}),

(0.A.123)

where

\displaystyle E(N,{\varphi},\mathcal{I},\boldsymbol{{\xi}})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\frac{m_{N}}{NL_{N}^{4}}\|{\varphi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}\frac{{\xi}_{k}(x)}{N}

(0.A.124)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NL_{N}^{2}}\|{\varphi}\|^{2}_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\;\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}\left(\frac{{\xi}(x)}{N}\right).

We now bound the moments of the terms on the right-hand side of (
0.A.123
) with
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}={\eta}^{N}(t)
. For the first term, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and then by estimates (
0.A.120
) and (
0.A.122
), we have for all
r\geq 1
and
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(t))|^{r}\Big|\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big|^{r}\Big]

(0.A.125)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(t))|^{2r}\Big]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big|\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big|^{2r}\Big]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

To bound the moments of the second term on the right-hand side of (
0.A.123
), for
r\geq 1
, we take both sides of (
0.A.124
) to the power of
r
, use (
0.A.118
) and (
0.A.93
), and then Theorem
3.3
and the fact that by Assumption
1
,
\displaystyle\frac{m_{N}}{L_{N}^{2}}\rightarrow m\in(0,\infty)
and
L_{N}\rightarrow\infty
as
N\rightarrow\infty
to conclude that for all
r\geq 1
and
T\geq 0
,

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}[E(N,{\varphi},\mathcal{I},{\eta}^{N}(t))^{r}]=0.

(0.A.126)

By (
0.A.123
), (
0.A.125
) and (
0.A.126
), we have for
T\geq 0
and
r\geq 1
,

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big|\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big|^{r}\Big]<\infty.

(0.A.127)

Assertion (iv) then follows from combining (
0.A.120
), (
0.A.122
) and (
0.A.127
).

Proof of assertion (v):

Since the proof that the process
M
is a càdlàg martingale is similar to the proof of Lemma
4.2
(v), we will omit some details. It follows from assertion (iv) of this lemma and the triangle inequality that
M
is an integrable càdlàg process. It remains to verify that
M
satisfies the martingale property with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
. For every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, define
h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}:\mathcal{S}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by, for all
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
,

h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})\wedge n^{2}.

(0.A.128)

By assertion (i) of this lemma,
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
. By (
0.A.128
), for all
\boldsymbol{{\xi}},\boldsymbol{{\zeta}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
and every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq 2n\Big|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})-g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\zeta}})\Big|.

Therefore,
h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\in\mathscr{C}_{*}(\mathcal{S}^{N};\mathbb{R})
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, by Theorem
3.2
, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the process
(M^{(n)}(T))_{T\geq 0}
given by, for all
T\geq 0
,

M^{(n)}(T)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(T))-h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}({\eta}^{N}(0))-\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)({\eta}^{N}(t-))\,dt

(0.A.129)

is a càdlàg martingale with respect to the filtration
\{\mathcal{F}^{{\eta}^{N}}_{t+}\}_{t\geq 0}
. By the definitions of
g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
5.1
) and of
h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}
in (
0.A.128
), and by the fact that
{\varphi}
is bounded and has compact support, and by (
2.3
), by the same argument as we used to derive (
0.A.102
), the following limit holds almost surely for
T\geq 0
:

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\;M^{(n)}(T)=M(T).

(0.A.130)

As in the proof of Lemma
4.2
(v), by (
0.A.130
), the martingale property of
M
will be proved after establishing that for all
T\geq 0
, the sequence of random variables
(M^{(n)}(T))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly integrable. By (
0.A.120
) and the same argument as used to derive (
0.A.109
), it will suffice to prove that for every
N\in\mathbb{N}
and all
T\geq 0
,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\;\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big]<\infty.

(0.A.131)

By applying (
0.A.106
) and (
0.A.116
), and then the triangle inequality and (
5.18
), we conclude that for any
k\in\mathcal{I}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}
and
\boldsymbol{{\xi}}=({\xi}_{j}(y))_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\,y\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathcal{S}^{N}
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big|h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x+L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})|}{NL_{N}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}\|{\varphi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2},

(0.A.132)

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big|h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x-L_{N}^{-1})}_{k}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})|}{NL_{N}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}\|{\varphi}^{\prime}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2},

\displaystyle\Big|h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}+\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})|}{NL_{N}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2},

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{\{{\xi}_{k}(x)>0\}}\Big|h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}-\boldsymbol{e}^{(x)}_{k}\Big)-h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\left(\boldsymbol{{\xi}}\right)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2|g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}}(\boldsymbol{{\xi}})|}{NL_{N}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}+\frac{1}{N^{2}L_{N}^{2}}\|{\varphi}\|_{L_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}^{2}.

Combining (
2.6
), the triangle inequality, the fact that
{\varphi}
has compact support, (
0.A.132
) and then Fubini’s theorem, the definition of
F^{+}=(F^{+}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
in (
4.10
), and (
0.A.118
), we conclude that there exists
R>0
such that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,{\varphi}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t))\Bigg(\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}u^{N}_{k}(t,x)\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}u^{N}_{k}(t,x)\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t))\Bigg(\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t,x))\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t,x))\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg].

By applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[\Big(\mathcal{L}^{N}h^{N,{\varphi},n}_{\mathcal{I}}\Big)^{2}({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big]

(0.A.133)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N,{\varphi}}\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{4}({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}u^{N}_{k}(t,x)\Bigg)^{4}\Bigg]^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}u^{N}_{k}(t,x)\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[(g^{N,{\varphi}}_{\mathcal{I}})^{4}({\eta}^{N}(t))\Big]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t,x))\Bigg)^{4}\Bigg]^{1/2}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{\{x\in L_{N}^{-1}\mathbb{Z}:|x|<R\}}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}}F^{+}_{k}(u^{N}(t,x))\Bigg)^{2}\Bigg].

By substituting (
0.A.120
) and (
0.A.118
) into (
0.A.133
), and then applying Fubini’s theorem and then Theorem
3.3
, we get (
0.A.131
). Then, by (
0.A.129
), (
0.A.120
), (
0.A.131
) and the same argument as for (
0.A.109
), we have for any
T\geq 0
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\boldsymbol{{\eta}}^{N}}\Big[(M^{(n)}(T))^{2}\Big]<\infty,

which implies the sequence of random variables
(M^{(n)}(T))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is uniformly integrable. As explained after (
0.A.130
), combining (
0.A.130
) and the uniform integrability of
(M^{(n)}(T))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
implies that
M
is a martingale, which completes the proof.
∎

0.A.7
Weak selection–low mutation regime

In this subsection, we will establish the limit in (
2.24
).

Lemma 0.A.13
.

Let
s,{\mu}\in(0,\infty)
and
(s^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}},({\mu}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset(0,1)
be such that (
2.21
) holds. For
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, let
{\alpha}^{(n)}_{k}
be defined as in (
2.22
),
\hat{{\alpha}}_{k}
be defined as in (
2.23
), and let
{\alpha}^{(n)}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=({\alpha}^{(n)}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
and
\hat{{\alpha}}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=(\hat{{\alpha}}_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
. Then
({\alpha}^{(n)})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in
{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
to
\hat{{\alpha}}
.

Proof.

We first establish bounds, uniform in
n\in\mathbb{N}
, on
\|{\alpha}^{(n)}\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}
and on the tails of
{\alpha}^{(n)}
. We start by recalling the elementary inequalities

1+y\leq e^{y}\quad\forall\,y\in\mathbb{R},

(0.A.134)

and

1-e^{-y}\geq(1-e^{-1})(y\wedge 1)\quad\forall\,y\in[0,\infty).

(0.A.135)

By (
0.A.134
) and then by (
0.A.135
), we have that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle 1-(1-s^{(n)})^{i}\geq 1-e^{-is^{(n)}}\geq(1-e^{-1})(is^{(n)}\wedge 1).

(0.A.136)

Observe that by (
2.21
), there exists
n^{*}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

{\mu}^{(n)}<e^{-1}(1-e^{-1})\quad\forall\,n\geq n^{*}.

(0.A.137)

In particular,

1-{\mu}^{(n)}>1-e^{-1}(1-e^{-1})>e^{-1}\quad\forall\,n\geq n^{*}.

(0.A.138)

Using (
2.22
), and then the fact that
s^{(n)},{\mu}^{(n)}\in(0,1)
, (
0.A.136
) and (
0.A.138
), we conclude that for
n\geq n^{*}
,

\displaystyle\|{\alpha}^{(n)}\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{{\mu}^{(n)}}{1-{\mu}^{(n)}}\right)^{k}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\frac{(1-s^{(n)})^{i-1}}{(1-(1-s^{(n)})^{i})}

(0.A.139)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}({\mu}^{(n)}e)^{k}\prod^{k}_{i=1}\frac{1}{(1-e^{-1})(is^{(n)}\wedge 1)}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\Bigg(\frac{{\mu}^{(n)}e}{s^{(n)}(1-e^{-1})}\Bigg)^{k}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{{\mu}^{(n)}e}{1-e^{-1}}\right)^{k}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\frac{{\mu}^{(n)}e}{s^{(n)}(1-e^{-1})}\right)+\frac{e^{-1}(1-e^{-1})}{e^{-1}(1-e^{-1})-{\mu}^{(n)}},

where for the last line we used (
0.A.137
). Applying (
2.21
) to (
0.A.139
), we conclude that

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|{\alpha}^{(n)}\|_{{\mathord{\hbox{\char 96\relax}}}_{1}}<\infty.

(0.A.140)

By an argument similar to the one we used to establish (
0.A.139
), and using (
2.21
), there exists
C>0
such that for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
and
n\geq n^{*}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=j}^{\infty}{\alpha}^{(n)}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=j}^{\infty}\Bigg(\frac{1}{k!}\Bigg(\frac{{\mu}^{(n)}e}{s^{(n)}(1-e^{-1})}\Bigg)^{k}+\left(\frac{{\mu}^{(n)}e}{1-e^{-1}}\right)^{k}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=j}^{\infty}\Bigg(\frac{1}{k!}C^{k}\frac{e^{k}}{(1-e^{-1})^{k}}+\left(\frac{{\mu}^{(n)}e}{1-e^{-1}}\right)^{j}\left(\frac{e^{-1}(1-e^{-1})}{e^{-1}(1-e^{-1})-{\mu}^{(n)}}\right)\Bigg).

Therefore, we conclude from (
2.21
) that for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, there exists
j({\varepsilon})\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
such that
j\geq j({\varepsilon})
,

\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\;\sum_{k=j}^{\infty}{\alpha}^{(n)}_{k}\leq{\varepsilon}.

(0.A.141)

By estimates (
0.A.140
) and (
0.A.141
), Lemma
0.A.13
will be proved after establishing that
{\alpha}^{(n)}_{k}\rightarrow\hat{{\alpha}}_{k}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. The limit is trivial for
k=0
. For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, observe that (
2.21
) implies that for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{i}\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

is^{(n)}-C_{i}n^{-2}\leq 1-(1-s^{(n)})^{i}\leq is^{(n)}+C_{i}n^{-2}.

Therefore, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, (
2.21
) and (
2.22
) imply that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\alpha}^{(n)}_{k}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{{\mu}^{(n)}}{s^{(n)}}\right)^{k}=\frac{1}{k!}\left(\frac{{\mu}}{s}\right)^{k}=\hat{{\alpha}}_{k},

which completes the proof.
∎
